Research & Engineering Lineage · Evolved-DRF

Distributed Systems Engineering

The useful inheritance is disciplined reasoning about partial failure, uncertain outcomes and separated effects—not a mandate for microservices, consensus or cloud machinery.

Source layer: this page retains the original four-family research corpus and its repository-specific v0.4 crosswalk. Its property counts, accepted translations and retained gaps belong to that recorded target scope. The current DRF family record separately identifies corrected scientific subjects, denominators and later receiving. This historical interpretation does not requalify current native implementation.

Genealogy

Distributed-systems engineering did not begin as one architecture style. Early network and message-passing work made communication between independently executing processes explicit. Transaction, logging and recovery research asked how several state changes could be made coherent. Lamport’s causal order separated “could have influenced” from wall-clock order; snapshot work showed how to capture a consistent cut under stated channel assumptions. Replication and database research developed quorums, session guarantees, isolation levels, epidemic repair, causal consistency and convergent data structures for different histories and availability needs.

A separate agreement and membership lineage produced Byzantine agreement, the impossibility of guaranteed deterministic consensus progress in full asynchrony, partial-synchrony models, failure detectors, Viewstamped Replication, Paxos and Raft. These results did not say that every distributed state needs consensus. They clarified what agreement can protect, which timing and membership assumptions it needs, and why safety, progress and semantic validity are different claims.

Remote procedure calls and durable messaging exposed another problem: after a timeout, an operation may have failed, may still be running, or may have committed while its reply was lost. Later logs, stream processors and durable workflow systems improved replay and processing guarantees inside declared boundaries, but did not turn an acknowledgement into proof of an external business effect. Sagas and compensation developed alongside atomic transactions rather than simply replacing them.

Production systems then made capacity and recovery central. Queues, retries and failover can amplify load; replication can propagate corruption; telemetry can be incomplete precisely during failure. Modern overload control, metastability research, incident analysis and hypothesis-bound fault injection therefore extend the older protocol lineages with finite-capacity, common-cause, restoration and observation concerns. Cloud-native and agentic systems are later translations of these problems, not exemptions from them.

Selected sources and claim limits

SourceSupportsDoes not establish
Lamport, Time, Clocks, and the Ordering of Events in a Distributed SystemHappens-before and logical clocks distinguish causal partial order from an imposed total order.That a timestamp is a complete, current or semantically correct history.
Fischer, Lynch and Paterson, Impossibility of Distributed Consensus with One Faulty ProcessA formal non-termination result for deterministic consensus in a fully asynchronous model with one crash failure.That practical consensus is impossible, or that safety fails; real systems add timing and failure-detector assumptions.
Gilbert and Lynch, Brewer’s Conjecture and the Feasibility of Consistent, Available, Partition-Tolerant Web ServicesA partition-case impossibility for particular definitions of atomic consistency and availability.A permanent “pick any two” menu or one design answer for normal operation and recovery.
Shapiro et al., A Comprehensive Study of Convergent and Commutative Replicated Data TypesConvergence conditions for state- and operation-based replicated data types under stated delivery assumptions.Semantic validity, bounded staleness, or coordination freedom for an invariant that is not preserved by the admitted operations.
Birrell and Nelson, Implementing Remote Procedure CallsRetransmission, duplicate suppression and crash uncertainty at a remote-call boundary.Exactly-once external effects or proof that a timeout means non-execution.
Huang et al., Metastable Failures in the WildObserved amplification mechanisms across 22 failures reported by 11 organisations.A universal prevalence estimate or one overload-control prescription.
Ganesan et al., Redundancy Does Not Imply Fault ToleranceSingle storage faults causing loss, corruption, unavailability or propagation across eight replicated storage systems.That replication is useless; it shows that the tolerated fault set and repair behaviour must be tested.

What survives as engineering practice

The tradition’s first contribution is epistemic: a remote timeout is an unknown outcome, not a negative answer. A command exit, queue acknowledgement, local log entry or workflow status proves only the boundary that produced it. Completion requires a consumer-relevant postcondition, or the state remains explicitly partial or unknown.

Current membership and fenced effects protect consequential mutation only when the protected resource rejects stale actors or repeated semantic effects.

  • State the process, network, timing, storage, restart, membership and common-cause assumptions behind each guarantee; retain a local call or local transaction when no independent failure boundary is needed.
  • Name causal order, log or sequencer order, commit order, event time and observation time separately. Use only the weakest ordering and consistency contract that protects the named invariant.
  • Bind authority to current membership, configuration and state position. A lease or elected leader is not sufficient when an old actor can still reach the protected resource; stale mutation needs resource-side rejection or semantic duplicate protection.
  • Carry one semantic operation identity across retry, restart and replay. Request identifiers, delivery labels and acknowledgements are useful only when their lifetime and effect boundary match the operation.
  • Coordinate non-compensatable, non-confluent invariants at the smallest sufficient boundary. Prefer partitioning, commutative operations, escrow or compensation only when their assumptions actually hold.
  • Bound queues, retries and deadlines by end-to-end capacity. Backpressure and admission control protect the system from positive feedback; a queue stores deferred work but creates no throughput.
  • Treat recovery as re-establishing authoritative state, dependencies and external postconditions. A snapshot, replica, backup, restart or failover event is only one step in that path.
  • Treat traces, dashboards and health endpoints as sampled, delayed evidence with known missingness. Challenge high-consequence recovery claims with representative, hypothesis-bound fault injection and explicit stop conditions.

These properties are held in tension rather than maximised together. Strong coordination may protect a uniqueness or conservation invariant but costs latency and progress under partition. Retries may help a transient safe operation yet worsen overload or duplicate an unknown original. Fast failover may restore reads while write authority remains unsafe. Rich tracing aids diagnosis but adds overhead and still cannot guarantee a complete causal history.

How the tradition changed, and what v0.4 translates

The mature tradition repeatedly narrowed its claims: wall-clock order became causal or uncertainty-bounded order; a lock became fenced authority; blind retry became semantic identity plus a budget and deadline; replica count became failure-domain and corruption-aware redundancy; “eventual consistency” became explicit convergence, session and semantic contracts; and backup existence became tested restoration with external-state reconciliation. Atomic transactions, sagas and coordination avoidance now coexist because different invariants and effect boundaries justify different mechanisms.

IMPLEMENTAUDIT translates the applicable parts into repository and tool work rather than implementing a distributed runtime. It keeps local source, hosted state and publication state as separate observations; retains partial or unknown effects; requires post-action readback; binds evidence to current repository, configuration and release identities; and reserves one decision owner when several contributors supply evidence.

The accepted v0.4 mapping adds bounded convergences: dependency-correct parallel work must join current results before the central decision is recomputed; one semantic writer owns that join; specialist workers remain under parent custody with authority and fallback defined; the composed plugin’s configuration, version and package evidence describe one atomic product population; and model judgement remains evidence-relative while deterministic tools establish only the mechanical facts they actually check. Those relations were verified on the exact v0.4 candidate. They do not prove the source tradition’s full failure, delivery, fencing or recovery contract.

What was already native

Before this research family was composed, IMPLEMENTAUDIT already distinguished repositories and external systems, separated source/build/package/install/deploy/public states, preserved pending effects, required fresh readback after mutation, bound claims to exact identities, and used rollback or explicit residual state rather than treating an attempted action as closure. Its direct path for small, local, reversible work also matches the distributed tradition’s strongest economy rule: do not create a remote coordination problem without a named availability, scale, ownership or lifecycle consumer.

That overlap is convergence, not evidence that IMPLEMENTAUDIT implements Paxos, transactions or a message broker. The mapping reuses native currentness, evidence, authority, dependency and recovery owners where their predicates match; it does not rename ordinary repository controls as distributed algorithms.

Rejected caricatures, universals and ceremonies

The lineage rejects default microservices, global consensus, permanent “CAP pick two” slogans, quorum or replica counts without current membership and failure-domain evidence, “exactly once” business effects inferred from platform labels, request-ID-only idempotency, distributed locks without fencing, unbounded queues, harmless-retry assumptions, complete-looking traces, random chaos exercises, and green health endpoints as proof of end-to-end readiness.

Consensus, queues, leases, retries, sagas, service meshes, event logs and fault injection remain useful in context. Their names or repeated use are not properties. No distributed runtime, consensus layer, universal retry loop, quorum ceremony, mandatory fan-out or agent-specific reliability claim was imported into IMPLEMENTAUDIT.

Evidence and generalisation limits

The frozen research population contains 54 examined properties and 102 sources, but that denominator is an analytical construct rather than a historical school or universal ontology. Formal results hold under their models; operational transfer separately depends on actual clocks, storage, membership, network behaviour, external effects, recovery paths and common causes. Incident reports reveal mechanisms, not controlled prevalence. Vendor documentation establishes declared implementation semantics, not independent effectiveness.

The recorded original v0.4 crosswalk records only partial native coverage for the general distributed failure model, duplicate-aware delivery, semantic idempotency, retry budgets, protected-sink fencing and full restore/reconciliation. Distributed-agent translation also remains unresolved. This page therefore does not claim consensus, linearizability, fault tolerance, exactly-once effects, arbitrary-writer exclusion, continuous availability or universal distributed safety. The v0.4 verification establishes preservation of named architecture relationships on one exact candidate, not activation or effectiveness across every repository, host or external service.

P01 — Explicit distributed failure, timing, network, storage and recovery model
PROPERTY_ID

P01

PROPERTY_NAME

Explicit distributed failure, timing, network, storage and recovery model

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Byzantine agreement (1982), asynchronous impossibility (1985), partial synchrony (1988) and failure-detector theory (1996).

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  2. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

State the process, communication, clock and storage faults an algorithm tolerates and the timing assumptions under which it progresses.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Protocol names are often repeated after the assumptions that make their guarantees true have been dropped.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Crash, omission, delay, partition, corruption, clock and recovery faults are conflated; a proved algorithm is deployed outside its model.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Invalid inference from algorithm identity to deployed safety, liveness, consistency or recoverability.

MECHANISM

Attach every guarantee to an explicit model; check implementation primitives, failure domains and recovery transitions against it.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any correctness or completion claim spanning independently failing processes, stores, networks or administrative components.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

A local call, local transaction or one durable process when no independent remote failure boundary is required.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Known component boundary; declared synchrony; stable-storage semantics; restart behaviour; membership; clock assumptions; common-mode inventory.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-stop, crash-recovery, omission, delay, partition, storage corruption, clock error and correlated/common-mode failure as applicable.

TIMING_MODEL

The claim states whether it is asynchronous, partially synchronous, synchronous, or merely bounded by a local policy timeout.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

No consistency claim is accepted without a named history/observation model and operation boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Membership, configuration and ownership identity are explicit wherever they affect the claim.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Loss, duplication, reordering and retry are either in scope or explicitly excluded.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Restart, stable-storage, rollback and rejoin semantics are declared.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Evidence distinguishes suspicion from established failure and reports coverage gaps.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents model-free folklore, focuses tests on the actual fault envelope and makes evidence comparable.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Architect, protocol implementer, operator, reviewer and consumer of a distributed guarantee.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Storage corruption omitted

  2. Timing bound assumed but unmonitored

  3. Recovery changes membership outside the proof

  4. Common control plane defeats independence

  5. Implementation primitive is weaker than the model

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Models can be unrealistically clean

  2. Enumerating assumptions is not proof they hold

  3. Byzantine language can distract from ordinary crash/common-mode failures

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From generic 'node failure' to layered profiles covering process, network, clock, storage, restart, configuration, operator and common-mode coupling.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

An assumption contract travels with each guarantee, test and recovery path, and names the local cheap path.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Using a protocol or topology label is ceremony; the retained property is the checked model.

P02 — Partial failure and unknown-outcome semantics
PROPERTY_ID

P02

PROPERTY_NAME

Partial failure and unknown-outcome semantics

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

RPC retransmission and end-to-end semantics, failure detectors and later retry/reconciliation practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

A missing response means the caller lacks evidence about remote execution; it does not prove nonexecution.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

One component can continue or commit while another is slow, paused, partitioned, restarted or unable to receive the reply.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

The caller times out after the remote effect may already have committed or may still be executing.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Timeout-triggered duplicate action, abandoned committed work or unsafe failover.

MECHANISM

Represent UNKNOWN/PENDING outcomes; query by stable operation identity; reconcile, retry safely or compensate; propagate deadlines separately.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Remote mutation, distributed commit, task dispatch or external side effect whose response can be lost.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Local atomic mutation with a definitive return, or a harmless repeatable read.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stable operation identity, authoritative status/reconciliation interface, durable outcome state and duplicate-safe or compensatable effect.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-stop, crash-recovery, omission, delay, partition, storage corruption, clock error and correlated/common-mode failure as applicable.

TIMING_MODEL

The claim states whether it is asynchronous, partially synchronous, synchronous, or merely bounded by a local policy timeout.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

No consistency claim is accepted without a named history/observation model and operation boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Membership, configuration and ownership identity are explicit wherever they affect the claim.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Loss, duplication, reordering and retry are either in scope or explicitly excluded.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Restart, stable-storage, rollback and rejoin semantics are declared.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Evidence distinguishes suspicion from established failure and reports coverage gaps.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes epistemic uncertainty explicit and prevents timeout from becoming a false semantic verdict.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Caller, orchestrator, operator and business decision deciding whether to retry, wait or compensate.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Retry races the original slow execution

  2. Cancellation stops waiting but not work

  3. Status record is lost with worker

  4. Operation identity reused for changed parameters

  5. Unknown is prematurely mapped to failed

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Unknown-state handling adds latency and durable state

  2. Some external effects cannot be queried or reversed

  3. Aggressive timeouts can create the uncertainty they detect

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From RPC retry rules to durable unknown states, semantic operation identity, deadline propagation and reconciliation.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Timeout/no response yields UNKNOWN until authoritative postcondition, idempotent closure or compensation resolves it.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Turning timeout into an error code is not the property.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

failure

P03 — Failure-domain independence and common-mode awareness
PROPERTY_ID

P03

PROPERTY_NAME

Failure-domain independence and common-mode awareness

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Primary/backup and quorum replication, refined by geo-distributed placement and field studies of correlated failure.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  3. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Place redundant state and decision paths across failure domains independent for the failures being claimed.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Replicas share power, network, storage software, configuration, control plane, credentials, operator or region.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

A common cause removes or corrupts nominally redundant copies and the recovery/control path.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Replica count being mistaken for independent availability or durability coverage.

MECHANISM

Map physical and logical shared fate; place replicas/witnesses/control paths accordingly; bound corruption propagation and test correlated failure.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Replication, quorum, backup, multi-zone/region, control-plane or failover claim.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One durable copy plus tested backup where hot availability is unnecessary and consequence permits it.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Failure-domain inventory, placement evidence, recovery-path independence, integrity checks and out-of-band access.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-stop, crash-recovery, omission, delay, partition, storage corruption, clock error and correlated/common-mode failure as applicable.

TIMING_MODEL

The claim states whether it is asynchronous, partially synchronous, synchronous, or merely bounded by a local policy timeout.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

No consistency claim is accepted without a named history/observation model and operation boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Membership, configuration and ownership identity are explicit wherever they affect the claim.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Loss, duplication, reordering and retry are either in scope or explicitly excluded.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Restart, stable-storage, rollback and rejoin semantics are declared.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Evidence distinguishes suspicion from established failure and reports coverage gaps.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Turns 'N replicas' into a defensible fault-coverage claim and reveals shared recovery dependencies.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Storage/platform owner, resilience architect, incident commander and recovery planner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Bad configuration reaches all replicas

  2. Repair propagates corruption

  3. Witness shares failed network

  4. Recovery credentials/DNS depend on failed system

  5. Administrative labels overstate independence

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Perfect independence is impossible

  2. Cross-region independence costs latency and complexity

  3. Provider labels are not empirical proof

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From hardware redundancy to multi-layer common-mode analysis including software, configuration, control and operator.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Claim tolerance only for explicit independent domains; include data, authority, observability and recovery paths.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Three replicas or three zones is ceremony without a shared-fate model.

P04 — Causal order distinguished from wall-clock and arbitrary total order
PROPERTY_ID

P04

PROPERTY_NAME

Causal order distinguished from wall-clock and arbitrary total order

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Lamport's happens-before relation (1978), vector clocks, causal multicast and causal consistency.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CAUSALITY_AND_TIME_LINEAGE

  2. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Represent causal precedence and concurrency without assuming a perfect global clock.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Wall-clock skew, log merge order or arbitrary tie-breaking reverses or invents dependencies.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Events are delayed, reordered and concurrent across independently progressing processes.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Conflict resolution, replay, audit or a decision using an order that does not preserve the relation it needs.

MECHANISM

Use logical/vector/dependency clocks, causal delivery or a deliberately authoritative sequencer; preserve concurrency instead of fabricating a winner.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Correctness depends on whether one event could have influenced another, read-your-writes or dependency application.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Single-thread/local transaction order, or independent commutative operations with no causal consumer.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stable event identity, propagation of dependency metadata, stated sequencer/log semantics and retention of causal context.

FAILURE_MODEL

Delay, reordering, concurrency, partition, clock skew/jumps, replica lag and incomplete propagation.

TIMING_MODEL

No exact global clock is assumed unless a measured uncertainty bound and failure response are part of the design.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Causal, session, linearizable, snapshot or bounded-staleness semantics are named per operation.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Sequencers, leases and readers identify the current term/configuration where that affects currentness.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Event identity and dependency metadata survive retry/replay; transport order is not promoted to causal order.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay and failover preserve required dependencies and do not present older state as newer without signalling.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Clock uncertainty, lag, missing dependencies and sampled evidence remain visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents false temporal inference and enables safe concurrency where total coordination is unnecessary.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Database designer, event processor, workflow author, auditor and debugger.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Dependency metadata dropped at a boundary

  2. Vector metadata pruned unsafely

  3. Log order mistaken for real-world causality

  4. LWW chooses wrong semantic winner

  5. External-world causality is unobserved

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Causal metadata can grow

  2. Causality does not supply semantic conflict resolution

  3. Some invariants genuinely require total order

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From wall-clock/process-local sequence to explicit partial order, causal/session guarantees and hybrid logical time.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Choose the weakest order that protects the consumer; name causal, sequencer/log, commit, event-time and observation-time separately.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Adding a timestamp or correlation ID is not causal engineering.

P05 — Bounded-clock use and time-based authority with uncertainty margin
PROPERTY_ID

P05

PROPERTY_NAME

Bounded-clock use and time-based authority with uncertainty margin

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Clock-synchronisation bounds, leases and systems using bounded time uncertainty.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CAUSALITY_AND_TIME_LINEAGE

  2. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  3. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Use physical time only with a declared uncertainty/drift model; never infer exact global order or current authority from local time alone.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Clock drift, jumps, VM/GC pauses and uncertain network delay cause overlapping perceived leases or false freshness.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Different nodes observe incompatible time and liveness while delayed actors remain able to execute.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Stale owners mutating after handoff, premature TTL/dedup expiry and false timestamp order.

MECHANISM

Use monotonic clocks for durations, uncertainty intervals for ordering, conservative expiry, and resource-enforced generations/fencing.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Leases, TTLs, freshness SLAs, event-time windows or external-consistency designs.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Logical/causal clocks, non-time-based generations, or local timeout used only as a heuristic.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Measured bound, uncertainty excursion policy, pause/restart handling, lease issuer identity and fenced effect boundary.

FAILURE_MODEL

Delay, reordering, concurrency, partition, clock skew/jumps, replica lag and incomplete propagation.

TIMING_MODEL

No exact global clock is assumed unless a measured uncertainty bound and failure response are part of the design.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Causal, session, linearizable, snapshot or bounded-staleness semantics are named per operation.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Sequencers, leases and readers identify the current term/configuration where that affects currentness.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Event identity and dependency metadata survive retry/replay; transport order is not promoted to causal order.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay and failover preserve required dependencies and do not present older state as newer without signalling.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Clock uncertainty, lag, missing dependencies and sampled evidence remain visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Allows low-latency time-based mechanisms while retaining explicit safety margins.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Database, lock/lease service, scheduler, cache and operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. NTP step or long pause violates bound

  2. Monotonic clock resets on restart

  3. Old partition renews/acts

  4. Uncertainty exceeded unnoticed

  5. TTL treated as proof of nonexecution

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Conservative margins reduce availability

  2. Clock infrastructure is common-mode

  3. Bounded time does not create semantic causality

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From timestamp folklore to intervals, monotonic duration clocks, hybrid clocks and fencing-backed leases.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Physical time contributes evidence only inside its bound; stale mutation is rejected by generation where correctness matters.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A timestamp or lease API without uncertainty and stale-writer rejection is ceremony.

P06 — Currentness, freshness and session guarantees as explicit evidence
PROPERTY_ID

P06

PROPERTY_NAME

Currentness, freshness and session guarantees as explicit evidence

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Session guarantees, linearizability, causal consistency and geo-replicated database practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CAUSALITY_AND_TIME_LINEAGE

  2. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  3. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Specify what a reader may observe relative to its own writes, causal dependencies, real time and a staleness bound.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A reachable replica is alive but lagging, in an old configuration or unable to substantiate currentness.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Replica lag, failover and partition let a reachable component serve stale or divergent state.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Stale reads triggering duplicate action, apparent rollback, unsafe failover or wrong decisions.

MECHANISM

Expose operation-scoped consistency/session/freshness contract; carry version/dependency evidence; route, block, fail or mark uncertainty when currentness is unproven.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Read result drives mutation, completion, failover, user-visible monotonicity or safety-sensitive decision.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Immutable content, best-effort analytics or explicitly stale cache with no authority claim.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Version/commit index or causal token, replica lag and membership knowledge, retention and consumer-defined tolerance.

FAILURE_MODEL

Delay, reordering, concurrency, partition, clock skew/jumps, replica lag and incomplete propagation.

TIMING_MODEL

No exact global clock is assumed unless a measured uncertainty bound and failure response are part of the design.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Causal, session, linearizable, snapshot or bounded-staleness semantics are named per operation.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Sequencers, leases and readers identify the current term/configuration where that affects currentness.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Event identity and dependency metadata survive retry/replay; transport order is not promoted to causal order.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay and failover preserve required dependencies and do not present older state as newer without signalling.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Clock uncertainty, lag, missing dependencies and sampled evidence remain visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Separates reachability from authoritative currentness and prevents stale-but-green state from driving action.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Application consumer, user session, operator, workflow and failover controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Lag metric is stale or clock-skewed

  2. Session token lost across service boundary

  3. Failover chooses older branch

  4. Read quorum uses old membership

  5. Cache age mistaken for source authority

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Stronger currentness costs latency/availability

  2. Freshness telemetry is delayed

  3. Many reads do not need the strongest model

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From consistent/eventual binaries to per-operation session, causal, linearizable and bounded-staleness contracts.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A read carries enough version/configuration evidence for its consumer, or is explicitly stale/unknown.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A green replica or recent timestamp is not currentness evidence by itself.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

causality

P07 — Consistency level and isolation as an operation-scoped contract
PROPERTY_ID

P07

PROPERTY_NAME

Consistency level and isolation as an operation-scoped contract

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Serialisability and concurrency-control theory, linearizability, isolation critique, CAP/PACELC, causal and highly available transactions.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  2. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  3. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Name the history property, operation boundary and failure behaviour instead of saying 'strong', 'ACID' or 'eventual'.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Different models prevent different anomalies and impose different latency, availability and coordination costs.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Concurrent operations and replicas produce histories whose anomalies are hidden by vague labels.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Application invariants being assumed protected by a weaker model, or stronger semantics bought without a consumer.

MECHANISM

Derive the model from invariants and observations; document permitted anomalies; test histories and failure cases.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Shared mutable state with concurrent actors or replicas.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Single-thread/local immutable data, or commutative/invariant-confluent operations tolerating declared anomalies.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Operation/transaction boundaries, anomaly model, client/session semantics, implementation verification and failure response.

FAILURE_MODEL

Replica divergence, stale reads, concurrent writes, partition, corruption, repair error, common-mode failure and obsolete membership.

TIMING_MODEL

Availability and convergence may be asynchronous; bounded staleness requires a separate bound and measurement.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The replica model, permitted anomalies, convergence rule and semantic invariant are explicit.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Replica set, writer authority and configuration identity are current and durable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replication messages tolerate duplicate/reorder according to the protocol and retain required version metadata.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Catch-up, repair, rejoin, compaction and corruption handling are tested.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Lag, divergence, repair source, membership and invariant checks are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Aligns coordination cost with the invariant and blocks consistency-by-brand.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Application/data designer, database owner, reviewer and consumer.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Snapshot-isolation write skew

  2. External effect not covered by DB consistency

  3. Failover violates session guarantee

  4. Vendor label differs from definition

  5. Converged state violates invariant

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Formal labels are hard to apply

  2. History checking samples workloads

  3. Stronger consistency can reduce availability without protecting a real invariant

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From ACID/BASE slogans to named per-operation models, session guarantees, invariant-based selection and anomaly checks.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Each operation states required history semantics, protected invariant and failure response; stronger coordination is localised.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Selecting a product tier labelled 'strong' is ceremony.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

replication

P08 — Semantic conflict resolution beyond replica byte convergence
PROPERTY_ID

P08

PROPERTY_NAME

Semantic conflict resolution beyond replica byte convergence

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Epidemic replication, Dynamo reconciliation, CRDTs and invariant-confluence/hybrid designs.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Concurrent updates must merge according to operation semantics and invariants, not merely produce identical bytes.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Partitioned or multi-writer replicas diverge; deterministic convergence can still produce a business-invalid state.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Concurrent writes, delayed replicas and reordering produce conflicts whose resolution is application-sensitive.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Lost updates, resurrected deletes, negative inventory, broken uniqueness or arbitrary LWW decisions.

MECHANISM

Use application-specific merge, CRDT with stated algebraic conditions, escrow/reservation, or coordinate the non-confluent invariant.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Offline, geo or multi-writer state where concurrent progress is valuable.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One current writer, local transaction or coordination when the invariant is non-confluent.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Operation algebra, identity/tombstone policy, causal metadata, invariant argument and compaction horizon.

FAILURE_MODEL

Replica divergence, stale reads, concurrent writes, partition, corruption, repair error, common-mode failure and obsolete membership.

TIMING_MODEL

Availability and convergence may be asynchronous; bounded staleness requires a separate bound and measurement.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The replica model, permitted anomalies, convergence rule and semantic invariant are explicit.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Replica set, writer authority and configuration identity are current and durable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replication messages tolerate duplicate/reorder according to the protocol and retain required version metadata.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Catch-up, repair, rejoin, compaction and corruption handling are tested.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Lag, divergence, repair source, membership and invariant checks are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Permits available multi-writer progress only where merge semantics are defensible.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Data-type/application owner and replication layer.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. LWW loses causally important update

  2. Tombstone collected before delayed replica returns

  3. CRDT converges to invalid state

  4. Schema change alters merge semantics

  5. Identity collision merges different intents

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. CRDTs do not solve arbitrary invariants

  2. Metadata/tombstones cost storage

  3. Middleware often lacks domain semantics

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From syntactic reconciliation to algebraic convergence plus separate semantic-invariant preservation and selective coordination.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Prove or test merge/invariant compatibility; otherwise use ownership, reservation or coordination.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'Eventually all replicas match' is not the property if the matched state is wrong.

P09 — Anti-entropy, convergence and repair with corruption/lag safeguards
PROPERTY_ID

P09

PROPERTY_NAME

Anti-entropy, convergence and repair with corruption/lag safeguards

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Epidemic database maintenance, read repair/anti-entropy, log catch-up and CRDT replication.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Detect and repair divergence after disconnection or partial write without blindly spreading bad state.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Replicas remain stale indefinitely; repair selects an obsolete/corrupt source or overloads foreground work.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Long-disconnected or corrupted replicas rejoin and exchange state under uncertain currentness.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Silent stale failover, data loss, resurrection or corruption propagation.

MECHANISM

Use version/digest comparison, authorised source selection, integrity/semantic validation, rate-limited repair and guarded rejoin.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Eventually or asynchronously replicated state with a lag/disconnected-replica path.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Synchronous replicated object with no lag path, or immutable artefact verified at write time.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stable identity/version, current membership, repair-source policy, integrity checks, capacity budget and tombstone horizon.

FAILURE_MODEL

Replica divergence, stale reads, concurrent writes, partition, corruption, repair error, common-mode failure and obsolete membership.

TIMING_MODEL

Availability and convergence may be asynchronous; bounded staleness requires a separate bound and measurement.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The replica model, permitted anomalies, convergence rule and semantic invariant are explicit.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Replica set, writer authority and configuration identity are current and durable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replication messages tolerate duplicate/reorder according to the protocol and retain required version metadata.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Catch-up, repair, rejoin, compaction and corruption handling are tested.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Lag, divergence, repair source, membership and invariant checks are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Restores intended redundancy and consistency without turning repair into a new failure amplifier.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Storage/replication operator and failover controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Read repair copies corruption

  2. Repair saturates foreground capacity

  3. Replica catches up from stale source

  4. Deleted data resurrects

  5. Obsolete member rejoins

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. No inherent convergence deadline

  2. Checksums show difference, not correct source

  3. Repair can create metastable overload

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From blind background copying to fenced, integrity-aware, capacity-bounded repair workflows.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Repair has source authority, bounded resource use, rejoin gates and post-repair semantic/integrity validation.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Running a periodic repair command is ceremony unless authority and result are established.

P10 — Quorum validity bound to current membership and configuration
PROPERTY_ID

P10

PROPERTY_NAME

Quorum validity bound to current membership and configuration

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Weighted voting, Paxos-family quorums, consensus and strongly consistent databases.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  2. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

An intersecting decision set is meaningful only relative to the same legitimate configuration and state/version.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A numeric majority forms from the wrong, stale, duplicated or non-independent member set.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Partitions and reconfiguration allow conflicting views of who may vote or serve state.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Two configurations each forming majority, stale state winning or restored identity voting twice.

MECHANISM

Version membership; bind votes to term/configuration/log position; preserve intersection through reconfiguration; validate durable identities and placement.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Replicated decision, leader election, lock service or strong database read/write.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One current durable owner or local transaction.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Authoritative configuration identity, unique durable member ID, persisted vote/log and proven transitional overlap.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-recovery, network partition, delayed/stale messages, duplicated identity, reconfiguration and—only when declared—arbitrary faults.

TIMING_MODEL

Safety and liveness assumptions are separated; progress usually relies on partial synchrony and a reachable quorum.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Agreement/order does not itself establish application semantic validity.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Terms, views, ballots, configurations and durable member identity bind every decision and mutation.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Protocol messages are term/configuration bound and stale messages are rejected.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Votes, log position, snapshots and configuration state survive restart without rollback of authority.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Current term, configuration, quorum and commit position can be reconstructed.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents majority folklore from masking split authority.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Consensus/database implementer, operator and failover controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Split configuration

  2. Reused node identity

  3. Witness shares failed domain

  4. Read quorum returns stale state

  5. Durable vote/log rolled back

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Larger quorums reduce availability and raise latency

  2. Dynamic reconfiguration is difficult

  3. Intersection does not validate application values

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From static vote counts to configuration-indexed quorum certificates and safe reconfiguration.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A quorum certificate identifies current configuration, epoch and state position.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'Three nodes, therefore safe' is ceremony.

P11 — Consensus safety separated from liveness and semantic validity
PROPERTY_ID

P11

PROPERTY_NAME

Consensus safety separated from liveness and semantic validity

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

FLP, partial synchrony, failure detectors, state-machine replication, Viewstamped Replication, Paxos and Raft.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Agreement/non-divergence, progress/termination and validity of the chosen value are distinct obligations.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A protocol remains safe but unavailable, or all replicas agree on a syntactically valid yet semantically wrong value.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Partitions, slow links and failures prevent progress while agreement remains intact; agreement can choose bad input.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Unsafe manual fail-open, false continuous-availability claim or consensus-as-business-oracle.

MECHANISM

State safety invariant, liveness assumptions and input/application validity separately; expose quorum loss rather than violating safety.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Replicated exclusive decision or ordered log whose divergence would violate an invariant.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Local durable owner, commutative/partitionable state or explicit manual arbitration.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Fault/timing model, durable vote/log, membership, deterministic application and input validation.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-recovery, network partition, delayed/stale messages, duplicated identity, reconfiguration and—only when declared—arbitrary faults.

TIMING_MODEL

Safety and liveness assumptions are separated; progress usually relies on partial synchrony and a reachable quorum.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Agreement/order does not itself establish application semantic validity.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Terms, views, ballots, configurations and durable member identity bind every decision and mutation.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Protocol messages are term/configuration bound and stale messages are rejected.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Votes, log position, snapshots and configuration state survive restart without rollback of authority.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Current term, configuration, quorum and commit position can be reconstructed.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Provides replicated decisions without pretending to guarantee continuous availability or semantic correctness.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Protocol implementer, state-machine validator and operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Liveness stalls under partition

  2. Manual dual-primary violates safety

  3. Nondeterministic state machine

  4. Invalid command agreed

  5. Snapshot omits term/vote metadata

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Consensus is overused

  2. Leader implementations can bottleneck

  3. Proofs may not cover storage/reconfiguration/integration bugs

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From 'elect a leader' folklore to separate safety, liveness, validity and implementation evidence.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Use consensus only for a named invariant; preserve safety during lost progress; validate values and deployment assumptions separately.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A leader dashboard is not the property.

P12 — Versioned membership and safe reconfiguration
PROPERTY_ID

P12

PROPERTY_NAME

Versioned membership and safe reconfiguration

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Virtual synchrony/views, Viewstamped Replication, Paxos/Raft reconfiguration and Vertical Paxos.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Membership change is a distributed state transition requiring continuity of authority and quorum intersection.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Nodes join, leave, rebuild or move while old messages, leaders and configurations remain live.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Multiple configurations remain actionable across partition, delayed messaging or restore.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Split brain, two quorums, data loss during replacement or resurrected stale member.

MECHANISM

Use configuration versions/epochs, joint/overlap or equivalent transition, catch-up before voting and retirement/fencing of old identities.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Dynamic replica set, shard movement, autoscaling or disaster rebuild participating in authority.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Static single owner or whole-system offline replacement under an exclusive boundary.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Unique durable identity, configuration log, state transfer/catch-up, overlap proof and operator-visible transition.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-recovery, network partition, delayed/stale messages, duplicated identity, reconfiguration and—only when declared—arbitrary faults.

TIMING_MODEL

Safety and liveness assumptions are separated; progress usually relies on partial synchrony and a reachable quorum.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Agreement/order does not itself establish application semantic validity.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Terms, views, ballots, configurations and durable member identity bind every decision and mutation.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Protocol messages are term/configuration bound and stale messages are rejected.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Votes, log position, snapshots and configuration state survive restart without rollback of authority.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Current term, configuration, quorum and commit position can be reconstructed.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Allows elasticity and maintenance without invalidating quorum/consensus assumptions.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Cluster manager, operator and shard/replica controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Too many members removed at once

  2. Joining member votes before catch-up

  3. Old identity reused

  4. Config state restored behind data

  5. Control and data planes disagree

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Safe reconfiguration can reduce availability

  2. Tooling often bypasses protocol

  3. Proofs assume reliable identity/storage

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From static groups to logged, epoch-bound, jointly safe reconfiguration.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Membership is authoritative replicated state; decisions and mutations carry configuration/epoch through handoff.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Editing a node list or scaling a controller is not safe reconfiguration.

P13 — Current mutation authority enforced by epochs or fencing
PROPERTY_ID

P13

PROPERTY_NAME

Current mutation authority enforced by epochs or fencing

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Views, terms, ballots, lease-based lock services and fencing-token practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Once-valid leadership, lock or lease must not permit mutation after authority transfers.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

An old leader or worker pauses/partitions, a new owner is chosen, then the old actor resumes.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Stale actors remain live and delayed writes arrive after handoff.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Split-brain mutation, duplicate external action, corruption during failover or concurrent maintenance.

MECHANISM

Issue a monotone generation/epoch; attach it to each mutation; protected resource atomically rejects stale generations.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Exclusive writer, task lease, distributed lock, failover, shard ownership or maintenance handoff.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Local mutex/process ownership; commutative operation; local transaction with version check.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Durable monotone generation, resource-side compare/reject, ownership-transfer protocol and effect identity.

FAILURE_MODEL

Crash-recovery, network partition, delayed/stale messages, duplicated identity, reconfiguration and—only when declared—arbitrary faults.

TIMING_MODEL

Safety and liveness assumptions are separated; progress usually relies on partial synchrony and a reachable quorum.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Agreement/order does not itself establish application semantic validity.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Terms, views, ballots, configurations and durable member identity bind every decision and mutation.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Protocol messages are term/configuration bound and stale messages are rejected.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Votes, log position, snapshots and configuration state survive restart without rollback of authority.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Current term, configuration, quorum and commit position can be reconstructed.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Converts advisory ownership into enforceable non-stale mutation safety.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Lock service, scheduler, storage/API owner and failover controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Resource ignores token

  2. Generation rolls back on restore

  3. External side effect cannot be fenced

  4. Actor obtains new token for old intent

  5. Multi-resource action fences only one sink

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Every sink must cooperate

  2. Physical/third-party effects may not accept tokens

  3. Authority service can be unavailable

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From leases and locks to end-to-end resource-enforced fencing and versioned handoff.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Authority evidence travels to every effect boundary; stale attempts are rejected or semantically neutralised.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Owning a lock object or seeing oneself as leader is not the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

CONSENSUS_AUTHORITY_PROPERTY

Shared software, configuration, control plane, credentials, operator and infrastructure are considered where relevant.

OBSERVABILITY

Current term, configuration, quorum and commit position can be reconstructed.

CHEAP_PATH

Local mutex/process ownership; commutative operation; local transaction with version check.

MATURE_FORM

Authority evidence travels to every effect boundary; stale attempts are rejected or semantically neutralised.

ORDER_OR_CURRENTNESS_REQUIREMENT

STALE_REJECTION

P14 — Single-writer or local-transaction cheap path
PROPERTY_ID

P14

PROPERTY_NAME

Single-writer or local-transaction cheap path

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Primary-copy and local transaction lineages, retained as an anti-overengineering conclusion.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Eliminate unnecessary distributed concurrency by placing an invariant under one current durable owner or one local transaction.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Distribution is introduced without a scale, latency, autonomy or availability consumer.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Remote uncertainty, coordination and recovery machinery are created where local atomicity would suffice.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Consensus/commit failure and coordination debt exceeding any benefit.

MECHANISM

Co-locate state and operation; use one durable writer/local atomicity; replicate behind the writer only if needed.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Low write scale, central invariant, tightly coupled data, modest availability demand or easy recovery.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

This property is the cheap path; distribution triggers only after a concrete consumer appears.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Clear ownership, local durability, tested backup and fenced promotion if automatic failover exists.

FAILURE_MODEL

Replica divergence, stale reads, concurrent writes, partition, corruption, repair error, common-mode failure and obsolete membership.

TIMING_MODEL

Availability and convergence may be asynchronous; bounded staleness requires a separate bound and measurement.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The replica model, permitted anomalies, convergence rule and semantic invariant are explicit.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Replica set, writer authority and configuration identity are current and durable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replication messages tolerate duplicate/reorder according to the protocol and retain required version metadata.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Catch-up, repair, rejoin, compaction and corruption handling are tested.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Lag, divergence, repair source, membership and invariant checks are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Reduces state space, remote unknown outcomes and operational burden.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Architect and application/data owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Owner becomes bottleneck

  2. Failover lacks fencing

  3. Local durability insufficient

  4. Async replicas lose accepted writes

  5. Ownership hidden behind load balancer

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Centralisation may fail latency/availability goals

  2. Future scaling can be difficult

  3. One process is not automatically durable

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From primary-copy replication to an explicit design-first cheap path.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Start local or single-writer; distribute only the dimension with demonstrated need.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A monolith is not automatically good, but distribution is not maturity.

P15 — Strong coordination for non-confluent invariants
PROPERTY_ID

P15

PROPERTY_NAME

Strong coordination for non-confluent invariants

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Distributed serialisability, atomic commit, consensus and invariant-confluence analysis.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  2. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  3. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Coordinate operations whose independent execution can jointly violate a named invariant.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Locally valid concurrent decisions violate global uniqueness, conservation, bounds or exclusive ownership.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Partitioned or concurrent actors each act on incomplete state and jointly break the invariant.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Double allocation, oversell, duplicate uniqueness, inconsistent atomic transition or divergent ownership.

MECHANISM

Use serialisable transaction, consensus, conditional write, reservation/escrow or authoritative sequencer over the minimal invariant boundary.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Demonstrated non-confluence, globally scarce resource or irreversible action requiring an exclusive current decision.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Independent commutative operations, ownership partitioning, escrowed budgets or one local owner.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Invariant definition, operation set, consistency target, current authority/membership, failure and recovery model.

FAILURE_MODEL

Concurrent updates, coordinator/participant crash, partition, partial commit, blocked in-doubt state, duplicate side effect and failed compensation.

TIMING_MODEL

Blocking and latency under partition/long-running work are explicit; timeout does not resolve atomic outcome.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Atomicity/isolation covers only the declared transaction boundary; external effects need another closure.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Participants/coordinator/owners and transaction generation are durable and current.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transaction messages and external calls have stable identity and duplicate-safe recovery.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Coordinator logs, participant states, compensation/forward-recovery ordering and manual resolution are durable.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Prepared/in-doubt/committed/compensating states and unresolved external effects are discoverable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Preserves a real invariant while containing coordination cost.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Application/data owner and the consumer harmed by invariant violation.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Invariant boundary incomplete

  2. External side effect outside transaction

  3. Global lock bottleneck

  4. Availability fallback violates invariant

  5. Consensus agrees on invalid input

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Coordination hurts latency/availability

  2. Some invariants can be redesigned or escrowed

  3. Blanket 2PC/consensus overcoordinates

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From global transactions and locks to invariant-scoped coordination and hybrid strong/weak designs.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Demonstrate non-confluence, then coordinate the smallest state/effect closure or redesign the operation.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Consensus everywhere is ceremony; invariant-scoped coordination is the property.

CURRENT_STATUS

STRONGLY_RETAINED

P16 — Coordination avoidance for invariant-confluent, commutative or partitionable operations
PROPERTY_ID

P16

PROPERTY_NAME

Coordination avoidance for invariant-confluent, commutative or partitionable operations

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

CRDT, highly available transaction and invariant-confluence lineages.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Allow independent progress when all valid independently produced states merge without violating the invariant.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Global coordination is used even though operations commute, are ownership-partitioned, escrow-bounded or merge safely.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Unnecessary cross-node coordination turns delay/partition into availability and latency cost.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Avoidable quorum waits, global bottlenecks and lost availability.

MECHANISM

Prove or test invariant confluence/commutativity; use CRDT, escrow/reservation, monotonic dataflow or partition ownership; coordinate only the non-confluent subset.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

High-latency or partitioned environment where operation semantics support safe independent execution.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Non-confluent uniqueness/conservation/exclusive authority or irreversible global effects should coordinate instead.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Operation algebra, merge rule, invariant, duplicate/reorder semantics, metadata retention and ownership boundaries.

FAILURE_MODEL

Replica divergence, stale reads, concurrent writes, partition, corruption, repair error, common-mode failure and obsolete membership.

TIMING_MODEL

Availability and convergence may be asynchronous; bounded staleness requires a separate bound and measurement.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The replica model, permitted anomalies, convergence rule and semantic invariant are explicit.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Replica set, writer authority and configuration identity are current and durable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replication messages tolerate duplicate/reorder according to the protocol and retain required version metadata.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Catch-up, repair, rejoin, compaction and corruption handling are tested.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Lag, divergence, repair source, membership and invariant checks are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Improves availability, latency and scale without sacrificing the named invariant.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Data/application designer and geo/offline client.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Incorrect confluence argument

  2. Hidden cross-object invariant

  3. Metadata collection breaks merge

  4. Schema change breaks commutativity

  5. External effect is non-commutative

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Application semantics are hard to formalise

  2. Complexity can move into reconciliation

  3. Not every converged state is meaningful

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From generic eventual consistency to invariant-justified autonomy and hybrid strong/weak access.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Coordinate only the non-confluent subset and retain strong operations alongside mergeable ones.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'Use a CRDT' is ceremony without invariant and lifecycle proof.

P17 — Duplicate-aware delivery semantics
PROPERTY_ID

P17

PROPERTY_NAME

Duplicate-aware delivery semantics

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

RPC retransmission/duplicate suppression, durable logs, stream processing and broker semantics.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Declare at-most-once, at-least-once and processing/effect semantics per boundary, including crash, rebalance and replay.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Messages can be lost, duplicated, reordered or redelivered after acknowledgement, visibility timeout or consumer failover.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Producer, broker and consumer fail independently; acknowledgement and state updates are separated.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Lost work or duplicate processing/effects caused by hidden transport defaults.

MECHANISM

Define attempt and event identity; persist acknowledgement/offset relative to state; design retry, poison and replay paths; make duplicates safe or explicit.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Asynchronous transfer, queue, event log, task dispatch or producer/consumer retry.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Direct local call/transaction or fire-and-forget telemetry where loss is accepted.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Durable event identity, producer/consumer state, retention, ordering partition and failure/rebalance semantics.

FAILURE_MODEL

Message loss, duplication, reordering, redelivery, acknowledgement loss, producer/consumer crash, rebalance and late replay.

TIMING_MODEL

Asynchronous delay is possible; visibility timeouts and retry deadlines are policy bounds, not outcome proofs.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

State/effect and acknowledgement/offset atomicity are explicit where claimed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Producer/consumer/task ownership generations are current for ordered or exclusive processing.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transport attempt, message, processing, business operation and external effect identities are distinct.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay, poison handling, dedup retention and unknown outcomes survive restart.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Attempts, duplicates, redeliveries, lag, acknowledgement boundary and effect status are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Prevents delivery labels from being promoted into false business-effect guarantees.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Producer, consumer, workflow and effect owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Ack lost after processing

  2. Offset committed before effect

  3. Effect committed before offset

  4. Duplicate outside dedup window

  5. Partition order mistaken for global order

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. At-most-once may lose work

  2. At-least-once shifts burden to consumer

  3. Platform-scoped exactly-once can be costly and narrow

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From transport retry semantics to end-to-end processing/effect contracts and transactional offset-state integration.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Name semantics at every boundary and independently close the business-effect boundary.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A broker checkbox labelled exactly-once is not an end-to-end guarantee.

P18 — End-to-end business-operation and effect identity
PROPERTY_ID

P18

PROPERTY_NAME

End-to-end business-operation and effect identity

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

End-to-end argument, RPC/request identity, transaction-log and workflow lineages.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Distinguish transport attempts, messages, tasks, workflow steps, business operations and external effects.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

One intent is represented by multiple retries/messages, or one key is reused for semantically changed intent.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Independent components retry and replay without a shared semantic identifier.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Duplicate charge/order/action, wrong deduplication, orphan status and unreconcilable effects.

MECHANISM

Assign stable semantic operation ID plus immutable parameter/effect fingerprint; map every attempt/effect; persist status at the authoritative boundary.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Retriable mutation, long-running workflow, external irreversible action or cross-service transaction.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Pure read, locally atomic mutation with no reply-loss concern, or disposable telemetry.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Canonical operation definition, parameter binding, tenant/principal scope, retention and effect ledger/status query.

FAILURE_MODEL

Message loss, duplication, reordering, redelivery, acknowledgement loss, producer/consumer crash, rebalance and late replay.

TIMING_MODEL

Asynchronous delay is possible; visibility timeouts and retry deadlines are policy bounds, not outcome proofs.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

State/effect and acknowledgement/offset atomicity are explicit where claimed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Producer/consumer/task ownership generations are current for ordered or exclusive processing.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transport attempt, message, processing, business operation and external effect identities are distinct.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay, poison handling, dedup retention and unknown outcomes survive restart.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Attempts, duplicates, redeliveries, lag, acknowledgement boundary and effect status are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Provides the join key for idempotency, reconciliation, audit and compensation.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Client, orchestrator, service and external-effect owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. New ID per retry

  2. Same key reused after parameter change

  3. Key scoped too broadly or narrowly

  4. Effect system drops metadata

  5. Retention expires before replay

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Global identity can cost privacy/cardinality

  2. Canonicalisation is application-specific

  3. Identity alone does not prevent duplicates

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From packet/message identifiers to parameter-bound business intent and effect ledgers.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Identity is semantic and lifecycle-scoped; every attempt and effect is linked without conflating changed intent.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Adding a UUID header is ceremony if semantics, scope and retention are undefined.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

P19 — Semantic idempotency, not request-ID ritual
PROPERTY_ID

P19

PROPERTY_NAME

Semantic idempotency, not request-ID ritual

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

RPC duplicate suppression, transaction identifiers, idempotent APIs and durable workflow retries.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  3. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Repeated execution of the same semantic operation must produce an acceptable state/effect, not merely reuse a key.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A request ID suppresses one duplicate path while downstream, external or changed-parameter effects still repeat.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Retries, redelivery and late arrivals reach several independently failing effect boundaries.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Duplicate payment, email, provision, deletion or state transition despite superficial deduplication.

MECHANISM

Bind identity to semantic parameters; atomically record accepted outcome with effect when possible; make operation naturally idempotent or use guarded state transition/compensation.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any mutation or activity that may be retried, redelivered, replayed or concurrently submitted.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Pure read, commutative accumulation with duplicate identity, or one local transaction.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Operation identity, canonical parameters, current precondition/version, durable result record, downstream closure and retention horizon.

FAILURE_MODEL

Message loss, duplication, reordering, redelivery, acknowledgement loss, producer/consumer crash, rebalance and late replay.

TIMING_MODEL

Asynchronous delay is possible; visibility timeouts and retry deadlines are policy bounds, not outcome proofs.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

State/effect and acknowledgement/offset atomicity are explicit where claimed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Producer/consumer/task ownership generations are current for ordered or exclusive processing.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transport attempt, message, processing, business operation and external effect identities are distinct.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay, poison handling, dedup retention and unknown outcomes survive restart.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Attempts, duplicates, redeliveries, lag, acknowledgement boundary and effect status are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes retries safe at the real effect boundary and exposes operations that cannot be made idempotent.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

API owner, workflow author, client and external-effect owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Key stored after effect

  2. Key collision across parameters

  3. Downstream call not idempotent

  4. Key window expires too soon

  5. Old success returned after semantics changed

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Natural idempotency is rare for many physical effects

  2. Dedup storage grows

  3. A cached response may hide changed external state

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From duplicate request suppression to semantic equivalence, atomic effect/result recording and end-to-end closure.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

The same operation identity and parameters cannot create more than the allowed semantic effect across retry/restart/replay.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'We add a request ID' is not idempotency.

P20 — Bounded deduplication and replay horizon
PROPERTY_ID

P20

PROPERTY_NAME

Bounded deduplication and replay horizon

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

RPC duplicate caches, broker retention, event logs and workflow histories.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

  3. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Duplicate suppression remains valid only while identity and result evidence outlive every permitted retry, replay and delayed delivery.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Deduplication records, tombstones, offsets or idempotency keys expire while old work can still arrive.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Long partitions, restore from old backup, delayed queues and operator replay exceed the assumed window.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Ancient duplicate effects, deletion resurrection and false 'new' work after restore.

MECHANISM

Define maximum retry/replay/rejoin horizon; retain identity/tombstone/result evidence at least that long; reject or manually reconcile older arrivals.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Finite dedup cache, TTL, log retention, replay, delayed replica rejoin or disaster restore.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

No retries/replay and bounded local execution, or effect is naturally harmless under repetition.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

End-to-end maximum delay, restore age, retention policy, compaction rules and explicit post-expiry consequence.

FAILURE_MODEL

Message loss, duplication, reordering, redelivery, acknowledgement loss, producer/consumer crash, rebalance and late replay.

TIMING_MODEL

Asynchronous delay is possible; visibility timeouts and retry deadlines are policy bounds, not outcome proofs.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

State/effect and acknowledgement/offset atomicity are explicit where claimed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Producer/consumer/task ownership generations are current for ordered or exclusive processing.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transport attempt, message, processing, business operation and external effect identities are distinct.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay, poison handling, dedup retention and unknown outcomes survive restart.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Attempts, duplicates, redeliveries, lag, acknowledgement boundary and effect status are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes exactly-once/effect claims falsifiable in time instead of implicitly permanent.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Broker/storage owner, API owner, workflow and recovery planner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Late message after TTL

  2. Old backup resurrects consumed ID

  3. Tombstone compacted before replica returns

  4. Consumer offset retained less than data

  5. Key eviction under load

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Worst-case retention can be expensive

  2. Unbounded partitions make finite guarantees impossible

  3. Legal/business horizons may exceed technical retry windows

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From implementation-local duplicate caches to end-to-end replay-horizon contracts tied to restore and rejoin.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Every dedup/compaction expiry has an explicit maximum-age assumption and behaviour for older arrivals.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A seven-day idempotency window is not a permanent exactly-once guarantee.
