P21 — Acknowledgement separated from verified external effect
PROPERTY_ID

P21

PROPERTY_NAME

Acknowledgement separated from verified external effect

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

End-to-end arguments, RPC ambiguity, transaction commit records and workflow completion semantics.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  3. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

An ACK proves only the exact durable boundary that emitted it; it does not prove the intended downstream or real-world postcondition.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Callers treat receipt, queue acceptance, worker DONE or DB commit as proof the whole intended action occurred.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Effects cross several independent systems and some acknowledgements are lost or emitted before downstream completion.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

False completion, lost external action, duplicate retry and unreconciled partial effects.

MECHANISM

Name ACK boundary; link it to operation identity; verify required external postcondition or atomically close/compensate the remaining boundary.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Multi-hop action, asynchronous processing, third-party/physical effect or workflow completion.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Single local transaction whose return covers the entire required state change.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Declared postcondition, authoritative observation, provenance, effect identity and uncertainty/compensation policy.

FAILURE_MODEL

Message loss, duplication, reordering, redelivery, acknowledgement loss, producer/consumer crash, rebalance and late replay.

TIMING_MODEL

Asynchronous delay is possible; visibility timeouts and retry deadlines are policy bounds, not outcome proofs.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

State/effect and acknowledgement/offset atomicity are explicit where claimed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Producer/consumer/task ownership generations are current for ordered or exclusive processing.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transport attempt, message, processing, business operation and external effect identities are distinct.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay, poison handling, dedup retention and unknown outcomes survive restart.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Attempts, duplicates, redeliveries, lag, acknowledgement boundary and effect status are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes completion meaningful to the consumer rather than transport-local.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Caller, orchestrator, business owner and incident responder.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Queue ACK before processing

  2. Worker DONE before durable effect

  3. DB commit before third-party response

  4. Success cached while downstream rolled back

  5. Observation is stale

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Independent verification may be expensive or impossible

  2. Observation can race later reversal

  3. Some effects are only probabilistically observable

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From transport receipt to end-to-end effect evidence and explicit residual uncertainty.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Completion is defined by the consumer's postcondition, with the evidence boundary and remaining uncertainty stated.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A 200 response, broker ACK or worker DONE is not completion by itself.

CURRENT_STATUS

P22 — Transactional messaging or outbox/inbox closure
PROPERTY_ID

P22

PROPERTY_NAME

Transactional messaging or outbox/inbox closure

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Transaction logs, reliable messaging, stream-processing checkpoint/offset integration and outbox/inbox practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  3. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Close the atomicity gap between local state change and message publication/consumption.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A service commits state but fails to publish, publishes but rolls back, or applies an effect and crashes before advancing acknowledgement.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Database and broker fail independently; network/retry separates commit from message acknowledgement.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Lost event, duplicate state transition, offset/effect skew and inconsistent downstream projection.

MECHANISM

Atomically write state plus outbox, or transactionally commit broker records/offsets and local state; consumers use inbox/dedup and replay.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

A local transaction must reliably cause or record an asynchronous message, or a consumed event must update state exactly once within the closure.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Direct local state change with no asynchronous notification requirement.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stable event identity, publisher relay/recovery, ordering assumptions, dedup horizon and explicit external-effect boundary.

FAILURE_MODEL

Message loss, duplication, reordering, redelivery, acknowledgement loss, producer/consumer crash, rebalance and late replay.

TIMING_MODEL

Asynchronous delay is possible; visibility timeouts and retry deadlines are policy bounds, not outcome proofs.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

State/effect and acknowledgement/offset atomicity are explicit where claimed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Producer/consumer/task ownership generations are current for ordered or exclusive processing.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transport attempt, message, processing, business operation and external effect identities are distinct.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay, poison handling, dedup retention and unknown outcomes survive restart.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Attempts, duplicates, redeliveries, lag, acknowledgement boundary and effect status are observable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Creates a recoverable handoff between local atomicity and asynchronous delivery.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service owner, broker/stream operator, projection and workflow.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Outbox relay publishes duplicates

  2. Consumer effect outside transaction

  3. Event order differs from state order

  4. Outbox never drained

  5. Schema evolution breaks replay

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Adds storage/relay complexity

  2. Still not atomic with arbitrary third parties

  3. Ordering across partitions is limited

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From distributed transaction aspiration to bounded local transaction plus durable handoff and duplicate-safe consumption.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

The state-to-message boundary has a durable recovery record; any remaining external boundary is explicit.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Having a broker and an outbox table is not closure if relay, dedup and replay are unowned.

P23 — Explicit transactional boundary and atomic-commit choice
PROPERTY_ID

P23

PROPERTY_NAME

Explicit transactional boundary and atomic-commit choice

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Transactions, distributed serialisability, two-phase/nonblocking commit and later deterministic/geo transaction systems.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Identify exactly which participants and effects commit atomically, and the failure/blocking model of that choice.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Atomicity is assumed across databases, messages and external systems that are not in one commit protocol.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Coordinator or participant fails between prepare/commit; partitions leave in-doubt participants or partial external effects.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Partial commit, contradictory participant outcomes and false rollback expectations.

MECHANISM

Use local transaction, 2PC/consensus-backed transaction, deterministic ordering or another atomic protocol only for participating resources; persist in-doubt state.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

A non-compensatable invariant requires all-or-nothing across multiple transactional participants.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One local transaction; or independently commit compensatable steps with a durable saga.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Participant durability, coordinator log, isolation, recovery protocol, timeout semantics and explicit exclusion of nonparticipants.

FAILURE_MODEL

Concurrent updates, coordinator/participant crash, partition, partial commit, blocked in-doubt state, duplicate side effect and failed compensation.

TIMING_MODEL

Blocking and latency under partition/long-running work are explicit; timeout does not resolve atomic outcome.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Atomicity/isolation covers only the declared transaction boundary; external effects need another closure.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Participants/coordinator/owners and transaction generation are durable and current.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transaction messages and external calls have stable identity and duplicate-safe recovery.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Coordinator logs, participant states, compensation/forward-recovery ordering and manual resolution are durable.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Prepared/in-doubt/committed/compensating states and unresolved external effects are discoverable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes atomicity real within a defensible boundary and exposes blocking/availability cost.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Database/application designer, coordinator and recovery operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Coordinator log lost

  2. Participant heuristic decision

  3. External effect outside protocol

  4. Prepared transaction blocks resources

  5. Isolation weaker than invariant

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. 2PC can block and add latency

  2. Blanket rejection ignores cases where atomicity is worth cost

  3. Blanket adoption extends scope too far

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From global transaction aspiration to scoped atomicity, replicated coordinators, deterministic ordering and hybrid saga boundaries.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Choose atomic commit for a named non-compensatable closure; otherwise prefer local transactions plus durable handoff/compensation.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'ACID' or '2PC' without participant/effect boundary is ceremony.

CURRENT_STATUS

TRANSACTION_COMPENSATION_PROPERTY

P24 — Compensation and forward recovery with semantic limits
PROPERTY_ID

P24

PROPERTY_NAME

Compensation and forward recovery with semantic limits

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Sagas and long-running transaction practice, later durable workflow systems.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. DISTRIBUTED_TRANSACTION_LINEAGE

  2. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Recover a long-running distributed operation through ordered compensating or forward actions when global rollback is unavailable.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Steps commit independently and some effects cannot be transactionally rolled back.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Later step fails after earlier durable/internal/external effects; compensation can also fail or be non-inverse.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Permanent unowned partial business state and false claim that rollback restores the past.

MECHANISM

Persist saga state; define compensations/reservations/semantic locks; order and retry them safely; use forward recovery when reversal is impossible; escalate residuals.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Long-running, cross-service or external workflow whose effects are compensatable or repairable but not globally atomic.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One atomic local/distributed transaction when the whole closure can and should commit together.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Effect identity, compensation semantics, ordering, duplicate safety, durable orchestration, current external-state observation and manual path.

FAILURE_MODEL

Concurrent updates, coordinator/participant crash, partition, partial commit, blocked in-doubt state, duplicate side effect and failed compensation.

TIMING_MODEL

Blocking and latency under partition/long-running work are explicit; timeout does not resolve atomic outcome.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Atomicity/isolation covers only the declared transaction boundary; external effects need another closure.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Participants/coordinator/owners and transaction generation are durable and current.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Transaction messages and external calls have stable identity and duplicate-safe recovery.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Coordinator logs, participant states, compensation/forward-recovery ordering and manual resolution are durable.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Prepared/in-doubt/committed/compensating states and unresolved external effects are discoverable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Provides a governed recovery path for partial effects without pretending compensation is a true inverse.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Workflow/business owner, service teams and operations.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Compensation is not inverse

  2. Compensation order wrong

  3. Compensation itself duplicates/fails

  4. External state changed meanwhile

  5. Irreversible effect remains

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Sagas expose intermediate state

  2. Compensations demand domain knowledge

  3. Complexity may exceed a scoped transaction

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From named compensating transactions to durable, observable, idempotent compensation/forward-recovery plans with residual-state handling.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Compensation states what it restores, what it cannot restore and how unresolved residuals are detected and escalated.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A compensating endpoint on a diagram is not recovery evidence.

P25 — Durable workflow state as a recoverable distributed state machine
PROPERTY_ID

P25

PROPERTY_NAME

Durable workflow state as a recoverable distributed state machine

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Sagas, workflow systems, durable logs and modern serverless durable execution.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

  3. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Persist enough workflow history/state that orchestration survives process loss and resumes deterministically or by explicit repair.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Request/worker memory is lost on restart; timers, child work, retries and compensation become orphaned.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Orchestrator and workers fail independently while tasks and external effects continue.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Lost long-running work, duplicate restart, forgotten timers/compensations and operator guesswork.

MECHANISM

Persist workflow transitions/history, task identities, timers, signals and terminal state; replay or recover from snapshot/log; expose manual intervention.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Long-running, multi-step, retrying or externally signalled work that must survive process/platform restart.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Synchronous local call/transaction whose whole lifetime fits one reliable execution boundary.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Durable ordered history, stable workflow/run identity, deterministic/versioned orchestration and external-effect reconciliation.

FAILURE_MODEL

Worker/orchestrator crash, task redelivery, lease expiry, orphaned work, replay, cancellation race, code-version change and external side-effect uncertainty.

TIMING_MODEL

Timers, heartbeats and leases are policy evidence; they do not prove prior execution stopped.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Durable workflow state is distinct from correctness/currentness of external systems.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow run, task attempt and worker generation identify current ownership; stale effects are fenced or neutralised.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Activity dispatch is assumed duplicable unless the entire effect boundary is atomically closed.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

History/snapshot replay, versioning, unknown-effect reconciliation and manual intervention are retained.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow state, task attempts, effect status and version are reconstructable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes distributed work state recoverable and inspectable rather than implicit in transient processes.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Workflow author, orchestration runtime, operator and business owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. History lost or corrupted

  2. External effect not in history

  3. Replay code changed

  4. Duplicate task after recovery

  5. Terminal state set before effect verified

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Persistence adds latency/cost

  2. History can grow

  3. Durability does not guarantee semantic correctness of external world

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From ad hoc retry scripts and sagas to durable state machines with history, timers, signals and explicit effect boundaries.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Workflow history is authoritative for orchestration, while external state is independently validated where required.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Deploying a workflow engine is not the property if workflow/effect state is not durable and reconcilable.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

workflow

P26 — Task ownership, lease expiry and duplicate-safe re-dispatch
PROPERTY_ID

P26

PROPERTY_NAME

Task ownership, lease expiry and duplicate-safe re-dispatch

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Leases, task queues, workflow activity retries and fencing generations.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

  2. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  3. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

A task may be re-dispatched only with a policy for the prior worker that may still be alive and acting.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Heartbeat/lease expires because a worker is slow or partitioned; new worker starts while old worker resumes.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Two attempts concurrently believe they own one task or external effect.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Double execution, conflicting writes, duplicate external side effect and lost progress.

MECHANISM

Give attempts stable operation identity and attempt generation; fence protected resources or make effects idempotent/compensatable; retain unknown prior attempt state.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Task queue, visibility timeout, worker lease, heartbeat or automatic re-dispatch.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One local worker under a process-local lock, or harmless repeatable calculation with no effect.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Task/run/attempt identity, current ownership generation, timeout policy, effect closure, heartbeat semantics and durable retry record.

FAILURE_MODEL

Worker/orchestrator crash, task redelivery, lease expiry, orphaned work, replay, cancellation race, code-version change and external side-effect uncertainty.

TIMING_MODEL

Timers, heartbeats and leases are policy evidence; they do not prove prior execution stopped.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Durable workflow state is distinct from correctness/currentness of external systems.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow run, task attempt and worker generation identify current ownership; stale effects are fenced or neutralised.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Activity dispatch is assumed duplicable unless the entire effect boundary is atomically closed.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

History/snapshot replay, versioning, unknown-effect reconciliation and manual intervention are retained.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow state, task attempts, effect status and version are reconstructable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Allows recovery from lost workers without converting slowness into duplicate harm.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Orchestrator, scheduler, worker and effect owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Lease expiry does not stop worker

  2. New attempt uses new operation ID

  3. External sink ignores generation

  4. Heartbeat delayed under overload

  5. Cancellation races completion

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Fencing every external effect can be impossible

  2. Long leases slow recovery

  3. Short leases create false failover

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From visibility timeout/redelivery to generation-bound attempts, unknown-state handling and effect-specific duplicate protection.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Re-dispatch is safe even if the prior actor resumes; the mechanism is fencing, semantic idempotency or explicit compensation.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A visibility timeout or heartbeat is not proof the old worker cannot act.

CURRENT_STATUS

P27 — Completion defined by durable state plus verified postcondition
PROPERTY_ID

P27

PROPERTY_NAME

Completion defined by durable state plus verified postcondition

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

End-to-end correctness, transaction commit semantics, sagas and durable workflow systems.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

  2. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

A distributed action is complete only at a declared consumer-relevant postcondition, not merely because a worker returned.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Workflow state says DONE while downstream, external or physical state is absent, stale or later rolled back.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Worker report, orchestrator commit and external observation occur in different failure domains.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

False terminal state, premature notification, duplicate re-execution and unreconciled real-world action.

MECHANISM

Define terminal invariant; persist transition; link required effect identities; independently query/observe or transactionally enclose them; record PARTIAL/UNKNOWN when not proved.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Completion drives user notification, billing, next workflow, resource release or irreversible decision.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Local transaction return covers the complete required postcondition.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Consumer-defined postcondition, authoritative evidence source, operation/effect identity, currentness and residual uncertainty policy.

FAILURE_MODEL

Worker/orchestrator crash, task redelivery, lease expiry, orphaned work, replay, cancellation race, code-version change and external side-effect uncertainty.

TIMING_MODEL

Timers, heartbeats and leases are policy evidence; they do not prove prior execution stopped.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Durable workflow state is distinct from correctness/currentness of external systems.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow run, task attempt and worker generation identify current ownership; stale effects are fenced or neutralised.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Activity dispatch is assumed duplicable unless the entire effect boundary is atomically closed.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

History/snapshot replay, versioning, unknown-effect reconciliation and manual intervention are retained.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow state, task attempts, effect status and version are reconstructable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes DONE auditable and meaningful, preserving uncertainty instead of hiding it.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Business process owner, caller, orchestrator and operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Worker report treated as effect proof

  2. Observation is stale

  3. External state changes after check

  4. Terminal state cannot be reopened

  5. One of several effects omitted

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Independent verification costs latency

  2. Some real-world postconditions are not exactly observable

  3. Continuous conditions can later become false

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From process return codes to durable terminal-state contracts with postcondition evidence and explicit uncertainty.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

DONE identifies the evidence and boundary establishing the required effect; otherwise state remains PARTIAL or UNKNOWN.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Green task status is not completion.

CURRENT_STATUS

WORKFLOW_STATE_PROPERTY

  1. S043

History/snapshot replay, versioning, unknown-effect reconciliation and manual intervention are retained.

RECOVERY_ASSUMPTIONS

History/snapshot replay, versioning, unknown-effect reconciliation and manual intervention are retained.

COMMON_MODE_ASSUMPTIONS

Shared software, configuration, control plane, credentials, operator and infrastructure are considered where relevant.

OBSERVABILITY

CHEAP_PATH

P28 — Deterministic replay with explicit workflow/code version evolution
PROPERTY_ID

P28

PROPERTY_NAME

Deterministic replay with explicit workflow/code version evolution

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Event sourcing, replay-based workflow runtimes and durable-functions semantics.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

  2. HYBRID

ORIGINAL_FORM

Re-executing orchestration logic against recorded history must reproduce compatible decisions, or migration/version logic must deliberately translate it.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Code, dependencies, nondeterministic APIs or schemas change while old histories are replayed.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Restarted orchestrator executes a different decision path from the one that produced persisted events.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Non-deterministic replay, stuck histories, duplicate task scheduling and semantic corruption after deployment.

MECHANISM

Restrict orchestrator nondeterminism; record decisions; version branches/patches; test old histories; migrate or pin incompatible workflows; separate external activities.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Replay-based durable workflow, event sourcing or state reconstruction across code versions.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Persist explicit current state without replay, or finish short-lived work before incompatible deployment.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Immutable history/event semantics, version identifiers, deterministic APIs, compatibility tests and migration/rollback plan.

FAILURE_MODEL

Worker/orchestrator crash, task redelivery, lease expiry, orphaned work, replay, cancellation race, code-version change and external side-effect uncertainty.

TIMING_MODEL

Timers, heartbeats and leases are policy evidence; they do not prove prior execution stopped.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Durable workflow state is distinct from correctness/currentness of external systems.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow run, task attempt and worker generation identify current ownership; stale effects are fenced or neutralised.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Activity dispatch is assumed duplicable unless the entire effect boundary is atomically closed.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

History/snapshot replay, versioning, unknown-effect reconciliation and manual intervention are retained.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Workflow state, task attempts, effect status and version are reconstructable.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Allows durable execution to survive code evolution without rewriting history accidentally.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Workflow/runtime developer, deployer and recovery operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Wall-clock/random call during replay

  2. Changed loop schedules new task

  3. Removed activity type

  4. Event schema incompatible

  5. Old binary unavailable for rollback

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Determinism constraints complicate programming

  2. Version branches accumulate

  3. External dependencies remain nondeterministic

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From replay assuming static code to versioned histories, compatibility APIs, migration and alternative speculative/current-state approaches.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

History, code and schema versions are explicit; every deployed version can replay or migrate all live histories.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'The engine replays deterministically' is not enough without code-evolution evidence.

P29 — Bounded queues with explicit work-age and capacity semantics
PROPERTY_ID

P29

PROPERTY_NAME

Bounded queues with explicit work-age and capacity semantics

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Queueing/service systems, staged event-driven architectures and production overload experience.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

  3. HYBRID

ORIGINAL_FORM

A queue is finite deferred work against finite service capacity, not an infinite availability reservoir.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Acceptance continues after completion capacity is exhausted; backlog age and memory/storage grow until service or recovery collapses.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Producers and consumers scale/fail independently; queue hides demand from upstream while downstream saturates.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Unbounded memory/disk growth, extreme latency, expired work, recovery surge and hidden overload.

MECHANISM

Bound depth/age/bytes; attach deadlines and priority; reject/shed/spill according to consequence policy; size from service rate and recovery capacity.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Asynchronous buffering between independently varying producer and consumer demand.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Direct synchronous flow with natural backpressure, or small fixed local buffer where overload consequence is acceptable.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Measured arrival/service distributions, deadline/value policy, storage limit, drain/recovery capacity and rejection semantics.

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence policy defines which work may be rejected, degraded, delayed or shed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Admission and priority policy has a defined owner; no implicit per-hop policy conflict.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Retries preserve operation identity and consume an explicit budget; shedding has visible consequence.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Backlog drain, retry release and recovery surge are capacity-bounded and observed.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Queue age/depth, utilization, demand, retry rate, deadlines, drop reasons and dependency saturation are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes queueing a deliberate latency/capacity trade rather than overload concealment.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service owner, queue/stream operator, capacity planner and business owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Depth bounded but age unbounded

  2. Disk fills

  3. Old work no longer useful

  4. Priority starvation

  5. Backlog overwhelms dependency during recovery

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Hard limits reject work

  2. Burst absorption needs headroom

  3. Capacity estimates vary and can be wrong

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From unbounded buffering to multidimensional limits on count, bytes, age and consequence, tied to drain/recovery tests.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Queue admission is governed by the probability and value of completing work before its deadline, with explicit shed/defer behaviour.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'We put a queue in front' is not resilience.

CURRENT_STATUS

OVERLOAD_BACKPRESSURE_PROPERTY

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

PROPERTY_FAMILY

P30 — Backpressure propagated to the source of demand
PROPERTY_ID

P30

PROPERTY_NAME

Backpressure propagated to the source of demand

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Flow control, bounded-buffer systems, stream processing and service-overload control.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

A saturated consumer communicates a rate/credit/refusal signal upstream before queues and retries amplify demand.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Local queues fill while producers continue at an unconstrained rate; overload moves rather than resolves.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Each hop sees only local capacity and can amplify or buffer requests independently.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Cascading queue growth, wasted work, timeout storms and downstream collapse.

MECHANISM

Credit/rate/window control, demand signalling, concurrency limits, propagated deadline/refusal, or producer throttling across the dependency graph.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Producer can outpace consumer or fan-out multiplies work.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

In-process bounded channel or naturally demand-driven iterator when no remote graph exists.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Current dependency topology, demand accounting, admission semantics, retry integration and fairness policy.

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence policy defines which work may be rejected, degraded, delayed or shed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Admission and priority policy has a defined owner; no implicit per-hop policy conflict.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Retries preserve operation identity and consume an explicit budget; shedding has visible consequence.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Backlog drain, retry release and recovery surge are capacity-bounded and observed.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Queue age/depth, utilization, demand, retry rate, deadlines, drop reasons and dependency saturation are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Aligns offered load with bottleneck capacity and stops overload migration.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Producer, intermediary, downstream owner and platform traffic controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Signal stops at one hop

  2. Retry bypasses backpressure

  3. Buffer masks saturation

  4. Control loop oscillates

  5. One tenant monopolises credits

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Coordination adds latency/state

  2. Topology changes

  3. Backpressure can reduce utilisation or create unfairness

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From hop-local flow control to dependency-aware end-to-end demand propagation and adaptive limits.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Every admission path—including retries and async queues—consumes an explicit capacity signal that reaches demand origin.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A local queue watermark with unconstrained upstream retries is not backpressure.

P31 — Admission control and load shedding with consequence policy
PROPERTY_ID

P31

PROPERTY_NAME

Admission control and load shedding with consequence policy

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Overload control, queueing/admission theory and production service practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

  2. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

  3. HYBRID

ORIGINAL_FORM

Decline, degrade or defer work before accepted load exceeds the capacity needed to preserve critical service.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Systems accept every request and fail all of them slowly, or shed indiscriminately without preserving critical/fair work.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Capacity and demand vary across dependencies; overload signals arrive late and partial.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Overload collapse, tail amplification, critical-work starvation and invisible business loss.

MECHANISM

Concurrency/rate limits, priority and fairness, deadline-aware admission, graceful degradation and explicit rejection/deferral semantics.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Demand can exceed sustainable service or shared bottlenecks can saturate.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Fixed small system with hard external demand limit and no shared saturation risk.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Capacity model, criticality/value classes, fairness policy, caller-visible rejection semantics, retry budget and telemetry.

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence policy defines which work may be rejected, degraded, delayed or shed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Admission and priority policy has a defined owner; no implicit per-hop policy conflict.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Retries preserve operation identity and consume an explicit budget; shedding has visible consequence.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Backlog drain, retry release and recovery surge are capacity-bounded and observed.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Queue age/depth, utilization, demand, retry rate, deadlines, drop reasons and dependency saturation are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Preserves useful work and system recoverability instead of maximising accepted-but-doomed work.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Traffic controller, service owner, product/business owner and clients.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Clients retry shed work

  2. Wrong priority starves critical work

  3. Admission signal is stale

  4. Global bottleneck unseen

  5. Fallback overloads another dependency

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Shedding loses service

  2. Priority can encode unfairness

  3. Capacity estimates and value classification are imperfect

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From generic 503/refusal to deadline-, value-, fairness- and dependency-aware admission with explicit consequence ownership.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Admission preserves a declared critical service set and tells callers whether to drop, retry later, degrade or escalate.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A circuit breaker that drops unknown work without consequence policy is not mature load shedding.

P32 — Retry budgets, exponential backoff, jitter and deadline propagation
PROPERTY_ID

P32

PROPERTY_NAME

Retry budgets, exponential backoff, jitter and deadline propagation

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

RPC retry practice, congestion/overload control and production guidance on coordinated retries.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Retries consume bounded capacity and time; they are not free availability.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Every layer retries independently, synchronously and after the operation's useful deadline.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Partial failures/timeouts cause many clients to retransmit into a degraded shared dependency.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Retry storms, duplicate effects, wasted work, thundering herd and deadline overrun.

MECHANISM

Centralise/allocate retry budget; cap attempts; use exponential backoff with jitter; honour end-to-end deadline; retry only classified/transient/duplicate-safe operations.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Transient failure can plausibly recover within the remaining deadline and retry is semantically safe.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

No retry for permanent errors, expired work, non-idempotent effect without closure or already-overloaded dependency.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Error classification, operation identity/idempotency, timeout distribution, downstream capacity signal, deadline and attempt accounting.

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence policy defines which work may be rejected, degraded, delayed or shed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Admission and priority policy has a defined owner; no implicit per-hop policy conflict.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Retries preserve operation identity and consume an explicit budget; shedding has visible consequence.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Backlog drain, retry release and recovery surge are capacity-bounded and observed.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Queue age/depth, utilization, demand, retry rate, deadlines, drop reasons and dependency saturation are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Retains availability benefit of selective retries without amplifying failure.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Client library, service owner, workflow and traffic controller.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Retries at every stack layer

  2. No jitter

  3. Timeout shorter than tail latency

  4. Retry after deadline

  5. Retry non-idempotent operation

  6. Hedging doubles overload

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Backoff delays recovery

  2. Error classification is imperfect

  3. Retries can hide systemic faults

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From blind immediate retry to budgeted, jittered, deadline-aware and semantically qualified retries.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

A retry is admitted like new work, consumes one end-to-end budget and carries the same operation identity.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'We use exponential backoff' is insufficient without attempt/deadline/idempotency/capacity bounds.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

overload

P33 — Metastable and cascading-overload containment
PROPERTY_ID

P33

PROPERTY_NAME

Metastable and cascading-overload containment

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Cascading-failure practice and the metastable-failure research programme.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

  2. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  3. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Prevent positive feedback from sustaining overload after the initial trigger disappears.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Backlog, retries, cache misses, failover traffic or recovery work reduces effective capacity and generates more of itself.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Independent components adapt locally in ways that amplify global demand and state transition.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Persistent degraded equilibrium, recovery failure and system-wide collapse.

MECHANISM

Identify amplification loop; cap queues/retries/concurrency; reserve recovery capacity; shed early; warm/ramp gradually; break feedback and validate exit path.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Fan-out, shared bottleneck, retry/cache/failover/backlog loop or recovery surge can feed on itself.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Low-utilisation local system with no feedback path and trivial restart.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Dependency graph, demand/capacity telemetry, feedback-loop hypothesis, safe shedding and tested recovery ramp.

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence policy defines which work may be rejected, degraded, delayed or shed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Admission and priority policy has a defined owner; no implicit per-hop policy conflict.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Retries preserve operation identity and consume an explicit budget; shedding has visible consequence.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Backlog drain, retry release and recovery surge are capacity-bounded and observed.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Queue age/depth, utilization, demand, retry rate, deadlines, drop reasons and dependency saturation are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Restores the ability to recover instead of assuming removal of the initiating fault is sufficient.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service/platform architect, incident commander and capacity owner.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Backlog drain overloads dependency

  2. Cache flush creates misses

  3. Failover doubles load

  4. Autoscaling lags

  5. Retries keep failed service hot

  6. Recovery releases herd

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Loops are difficult to identify before failure

  2. Mitigations may reduce normal utilisation

  3. Incident samples are selective

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From isolated component availability to dynamic-system stability, positive-feedback diagnosis and recovery-capacity reservation.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

The failure model includes amplification and a tested path out of the degraded state.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Restarting failed instances is not recovery if the feedback loop persists.

P34 — Dependency isolation and bulkheads with end-to-end verification
PROPERTY_ID

P34

PROPERTY_NAME

Dependency isolation and bulkheads with end-to-end verification

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Fault-containment, bulkhead/circuit-breaker and service-dependency engineering.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

  2. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Limit one dependency, tenant, queue or failure mode from exhausting shared resources needed by unrelated critical work.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Threads, connections, memory, CPU, queues or retry budget are shared across dependency paths and fail together.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

A slow/unavailable dependency consumes local resources while callers continue fan-out or fallback.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Cascading starvation and loss of all service due to one degraded path.

MECHANISM

Per-dependency/tenant concurrency pools, budgets, timeouts/deadlines, circuit state, isolation boundaries and tested degraded path.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Shared resource pool or dependency whose slowness/failure can block unrelated work.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One small dependency path with no shared-resource contention or acceptable full-stop behaviour.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Current topology, resource accounting, critical-path classification, fallback capacity and consequence policy.

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Consequence policy defines which work may be rejected, degraded, delayed or shed.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Admission and priority policy has a defined owner; no implicit per-hop policy conflict.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Retries preserve operation identity and consume an explicit budget; shedding has visible consequence.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Backlog drain, retry release and recovery surge are capacity-bounded and observed.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Queue age/depth, utilization, demand, retry rate, deadlines, drop reasons and dependency saturation are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Contains blast radius while preserving selected useful service.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service owner, platform runtime and incident responder.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Fallback shares same dependency

  2. Bulkhead too small

  3. Circuit opens on false suspicion

  4. Isolation boundary leaks shared DB/CPU

  5. Recovery herd after close

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Fragmented pools waste capacity

  2. Static limits age poorly

  3. Circuit state can oscillate or hide root cause

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From fixed pools and circuit breakers to dependency-aware budgets, adaptive isolation and end-to-end readiness tests.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Isolation corresponds to real shared resources/failure domains and has verified degraded/recovery behaviour.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A circuit-breaker library annotation is not containment.

CURRENT_STATUS

PROPERTY

Dependency isolation and bulkheads with end-to-end verification

FAILURE_MODEL

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

TIMING_MODEL

Deadlines and service-time distributions matter; old work may be worthless even if capacity eventually returns.

NETWORK_ASSUMPTIONS

Queue growth, shared bottleneck saturation, retry amplification, synchronized clients, head-of-line blocking, cascading failure and metastable positive feedback.

CHEAP_PATH

One small dependency path with no shared-resource contention or acceptable full-stop behaviour.

MATURE_FORM

Isolation corresponds to real shared resources/failure domains and has verified degraded/recovery behaviour.

PROPERTY_FAMILY

overload

P35 — Consistent distributed snapshot or checkpoint
PROPERTY_ID

P35

PROPERTY_NAME

Consistent distributed snapshot or checkpoint

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Chandy–Lamport snapshots, coordinated checkpointing and stream-processing snapshots.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

  3. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Capture a recovery cut that is consistent with message/channel and state transitions, not an arbitrary set of local copies.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Independent checkpoints combine states that never coexisted; in-flight messages/effects are lost or duplicated.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Components checkpoint at different times while messages and transactions continue.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Impossible restored state, orphan messages, domino rollback and duplicate/lost processing.

MECHANISM

Marker/barrier/coordinated checkpoint or equivalent protocol records local state plus required channel/log position; validate completeness and durability.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Recovery, migration, rescaling or audit requires a cross-component state cut.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One local transactional snapshot or reconstructable stateless workers.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Declared channel/order assumptions, checkpoint metadata, atomic durable write, log position and compatible application state.

FAILURE_MODEL

Process/storage crash, partition, inconsistent checkpoint, orphan message/work, log gap, corruption, stale failover authority and external-state divergence.

TIMING_MODEL

Recovery point/time objectives are evidence-backed, not inferred from backup existence.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The recovery line is internally consistent and reconciled with effects outside the log/transaction boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Restored configuration, ownership generation and membership are current; old writers are fenced.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay/redelivery uses original identities and does not duplicate unclosed effects.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Snapshots, logs, backups, restore tooling, capacity and reconciliation are tested as a full path.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Recovery progress, gaps, validation, divergence and residual unknowns are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Provides a coherent internal recovery point without stopping the system where assumptions permit.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Storage/stream/workflow runtime and recovery operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. External side effect outside snapshot

  2. Checkpoint corrupted

  3. Barrier stalls under backpressure

  4. Log truncated too early

  5. Membership changes mid-snapshot

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Snapshot consistency is model-specific

  2. Large snapshots cost I/O/latency

  3. Internal consistency does not guarantee external world consistency

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From global coordinated checkpoints to asynchronous barriers, incremental snapshots and log/snapshot hybrids.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Snapshot identifies membership/configuration, state versions, channel/log positions and excluded external effects.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Having periodic snapshots is not a consistent recovery cut.

CURRENT_STATUS

PROPERTY_FAMILY

recovery

P36 — Replay and restore with external-state reconciliation
PROPERTY_ID

P36

PROPERTY_NAME

Replay and restore with external-state reconciliation

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Write-ahead logging, rollback recovery, event logs and durable workflows.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  2. EVENT_LOG_AND_STREAMING_LINEAGE

  3. WORKFLOW_ORCHESTRATION_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Reconstruct internal state from snapshot/log and then reconcile effects not enclosed by that history.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Log replay finishes while third-party, physical, cached or downstream state reflects a different prefix or duplicate effects.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Internal durable history and external systems fail/recover independently.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Internally clean but externally inconsistent recovery, duplicate irreversible effects and hidden residual state.

MECHANISM

Restore a validated cut, replay with original identities, enumerate external effects/status, compare authoritative postconditions, compensate/forward-repair or mark unknown.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Disaster restore, log replay, workflow history recovery or regional failover with external effects.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

All state and effects are inside one local transactional restore boundary.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Complete logs, compatible code/schema, operation/effect identity, external query/ledger, authority fencing and manual residual path.

FAILURE_MODEL

Process/storage crash, partition, inconsistent checkpoint, orphan message/work, log gap, corruption, stale failover authority and external-state divergence.

TIMING_MODEL

Recovery point/time objectives are evidence-backed, not inferred from backup existence.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The recovery line is internally consistent and reconciled with effects outside the log/transaction boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Restored configuration, ownership generation and membership are current; old writers are fenced.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay/redelivery uses original identities and does not duplicate unclosed effects.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Snapshots, logs, backups, restore tooling, capacity and reconciliation are tested as a full path.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Recovery progress, gaps, validation, divergence and residual unknowns are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Turns replay into end-to-end reconstitution rather than internal state reconstruction only.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Recovery operator, workflow owner, application/data owner and third-party integration.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Log gap

  2. Replay duplicates external effect

  3. External state cannot be queried

  4. Schema/code cannot read old event

  5. Old writer remains live

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. External reconciliation can be manual and slow

  2. Some effects are irreversible/unobservable

  3. Logs may preserve bugs as well as facts

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From database restore to cross-boundary recovery plans combining replay, effect ledgers, compensation and validation.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Recovery closes or explicitly enumerates every state/effect boundary and preserves UNKNOWN where closure is impossible.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'Replay completed' is not externally consistent recovery.

CURRENT_STATUS

PROPERTY_FAMILY

P37 — Failover re-establishes current authority before mutation
PROPERTY_ID

P37

PROPERTY_NAME

Failover re-establishes current authority before mutation

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Primary/backup views, leases, consensus terms and disaster failover practice.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  2. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  3. REPLICATION_AND_CONSISTENCY_LINEAGE

ORIGINAL_FORM

Promoting a replacement is incomplete until former authority is excluded and the selected state/configuration is current enough.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

New primary is promoted while old primary can still write or the chosen replica is stale/divergent.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Partition and false suspicion create simultaneous actors with incompatible state and authority.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Split brain, lost accepted writes, stale takeover and divergent recovery branches.

MECHANISM

Establish current membership/state position, issue higher epoch/fencing, block stale resources/writers, validate replication/recovery point, then reopen writes.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Leader/primary/site/region failover or ownership transfer after suspected failure.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Manual offline recovery with verified old owner stopped, or one local process restart without concurrent actor.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Authoritative config/quorum, current data version, fenced sinks, durable generation, client routing and reconciliation of divergent writes.

FAILURE_MODEL

Process/storage crash, partition, inconsistent checkpoint, orphan message/work, log gap, corruption, stale failover authority and external-state divergence.

TIMING_MODEL

Recovery point/time objectives are evidence-backed, not inferred from backup existence.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The recovery line is internally consistent and reconciled with effects outside the log/transaction boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Restored configuration, ownership generation and membership are current; old writers are fenced.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay/redelivery uses original identities and does not duplicate unclosed effects.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Snapshots, logs, backups, restore tooling, capacity and reconciliation are tested as a full path.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Recovery progress, gaps, validation, divergence and residual unknowns are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes failover a safe authority transition rather than mere traffic redirection.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Failover controller, database operator and incident commander.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Old primary resumes

  2. DNS/routing sends writes to both

  3. New primary missing writes

  4. Epoch rolls back

  5. Application cannot tolerate cross-region topology

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Conservative fencing delays availability

  2. Determining most-current state can be expensive

  3. Manual disaster actions can bypass controls

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From heartbeat-triggered promotion to configuration-indexed, state-validated and resource-fenced authority transfer.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Failover completes only after authority fencing, current-state selection and post-failover validation.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'Leader elected' or 'traffic switched' is not safe failover.

CURRENT_STATUS

P38 — Tested restore and recovery-path evidence
PROPERTY_ID

P38

PROPERTY_NAME

Tested restore and recovery-path evidence

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. FAULT_TOLERANCE_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

  3. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

A backup, replica or runbook is only potential recovery; evidence comes from restoring the current system within consequence limits.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Backups are absent, corrupt, incomplete, too slow, incompatible or inaccessible during the incident.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Recovery tooling, credentials, network, schemas, logs and capacity fail separately from the production data path.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Discovering during disaster that recovery point/time objectives and authority reconstruction are fictional.

MECHANISM

Regular full restore/failover rehearsal using current artefacts, code, configuration, identities and realistic scale; validate data/effects/authority and measure residuals.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Any availability, durability, disaster recovery or rollback claim with material consequence.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Low-consequence disposable state that can be regenerated and whose loss is explicitly accepted.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Immutable backups/logs, independent access, compatible tooling, capacity, validation oracle, runbook and ownership.

FAILURE_MODEL

Process/storage crash, partition, inconsistent checkpoint, orphan message/work, log gap, corruption, stale failover authority and external-state divergence.

TIMING_MODEL

Recovery point/time objectives are evidence-backed, not inferred from backup existence.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

The recovery line is internally consistent and reconciled with effects outside the log/transaction boundary.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Restored configuration, ownership generation and membership are current; old writers are fenced.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Replay/redelivery uses original identities and does not duplicate unclosed effects.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Snapshots, logs, backups, restore tooling, capacity and reconciliation are tested as a full path.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Recovery progress, gaps, validation, divergence and residual unknowns are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Converts recovery design into current empirical evidence and exposes hidden dependencies before crisis.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Data owner, operator, incident commander and assurance reviewer.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Sample restore not full scale

  2. Credentials expire

  3. Restore tooling obsolete

  4. RPO/RTO omit replay/catch-up

  5. Recovered data not semantically validated

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Rehearsals cost time and resources

  2. Destructive tests need isolation

  3. Passing one scenario does not cover all common modes

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From backup existence checks to end-to-end restore, failover, reconciliation and return-to-service exercises.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Recovery evidence includes current artefact identity, measured RPO/RTO, authority fencing, external reconciliation and unresolved residuals.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A backup job marked successful is not a restore path.

P39 — Schema, wire protocol and event evolution under mixed versions
PROPERTY_ID

P39

PROPERTY_NAME

Schema, wire protocol and event evolution under mixed versions

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Database schema evolution, protocol negotiation, online upgrades and event-log replay.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. MESSAGE_DELIVERY_LINEAGE

  2. DISTRIBUTED_DATABASE_LINEAGE

  3. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Old and new producers, consumers, stored events and replicas must interoperate during rollout, rollback and replay.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Rolling upgrades create a mixed-version distributed system even when every binary is individually healthy.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Components observe incompatible schemas/protocols at different times and old events outlive the code that wrote them.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Decode failure, silent field loss, semantic reinterpretation, replay breakage and rollback impossibility.

MECHANISM

Version schemas/protocols; define backward/forward compatibility; use additive/staged changes; negotiate capabilities; retain old-reader/writer tests and migration/rollback plan.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Rolling deployment, long-lived event/log data, external clients, multiple regions or replayable workflow history.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Atomic offline upgrade of one local process/store with no concurrent old reader/writer and acceptable downtime.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Version inventory, compatibility matrix, contract tests using real historical data, field semantics and rollback/downgrade policy.

FAILURE_MODEL

Mixed versions, incompatible wire/schema changes, stale configuration, control-plane/data-plane skew and rollback incompatibility.

TIMING_MODEL

Propagation is asynchronous; 'flag set' or 'deployment complete' is not proof every component observed it.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Readers/writers define compatibility across old/new schemas and replayed historical events.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Current configuration/version authority and staged rollout state are reconstructable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Messages/events are versioned and unknown fields/semantics handled deliberately.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Rollback and replay paths are compatible with new writes; downgrade limits are explicit.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Version distribution, configuration convergence and compatibility errors are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Allows continuous change without turning version skew into hidden partial failure.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Service/API owner, data platform, deployer, client and recovery operator.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Field reused with new meaning

  2. Old writer overwrites new field

  3. New leader emits unreadable data

  4. Event replay invokes removed schema

  5. Rollback cannot parse new writes

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Compatibility layers accumulate debt

  2. Additive change is not always possible

  3. Syntactic compatibility can hide semantic incompatibility

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From stop-the-world migration to staged expand/migrate/contract, capability negotiation and replay-aware versioning.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Every live old/new reader, writer, replica and history has a tested compatibility path or an explicit cutover barrier.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'Rolling deployment succeeded' is not protocol compatibility.

P40 — Configuration and control-plane/data-plane currentness
PROPERTY_ID

P40

PROPERTY_NAME

Configuration and control-plane/data-plane currentness

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Versioned membership/configuration, online control systems and outage evidence from global configuration propagation.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CONSENSUS_AND_MEMBERSHIP_LINEAGE

  2. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Configuration is distributed state with identity, version, authority, propagation and rollback semantics.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

A control plane accepts a change while data-plane components observe different versions or a bad value propagates globally.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Asynchronous propagation, partial failure and rollback create configuration skew and stale control decisions.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Split policy, incompatible membership, global common-mode outage and false belief that a flag is universally active.

MECHANISM

Version and validate configuration; staged/canary propagation; monotone generations; convergence evidence; data-plane acknowledgements; safe rollback/kill switch; bound blast radius.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Configuration affects routing, membership, schemas, resource limits, feature behaviour or authority across components.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Local static configuration changed atomically with the single process.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Authoritative source, config version/provenance, validation, propagation topology, compatibility, rollback and independent emergency access.

FAILURE_MODEL

Mixed versions, incompatible wire/schema changes, stale configuration, control-plane/data-plane skew and rollback incompatibility.

TIMING_MODEL

Propagation is asynchronous; 'flag set' or 'deployment complete' is not proof every component observed it.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Readers/writers define compatibility across old/new schemas and replayed historical events.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Current configuration/version authority and staged rollout state are reconstructable.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Messages/events are versioned and unknown fields/semantics handled deliberately.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Rollback and replay paths are compatible with new writes; downgrade limits are explicit.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Version distribution, configuration convergence and compatibility errors are visible.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes configuration changes observable, reversible state transitions rather than instantaneous intent.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Control-plane owner, deployer, operator and every data-plane consumer.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Control plane says applied while nodes stale

  2. Bad config replicated globally

  3. Rollback incompatible with new data

  4. Node accepts old generation

  5. Recovery tool depends on failed control plane

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Staged rollout slows change

  2. Canaries may not represent global edge cases

  3. More control logic is itself a failure source

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From file distribution to versioned, observed, staged and bounded configuration state with currentness checks.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Configuration change completes only when intended population/version is established, incompatible nodes are handled and rollback remains valid.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

'The feature flag is set' is not evidence every replica observed it.

P41 — Current dependency topology and end-to-end readiness
PROPERTY_ID

P41

PROPERTY_NAME

Current dependency topology and end-to-end readiness

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Distributed monitoring, service-dependency practice and outages where local health diverged from usable service.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. OVERLOAD_AND_SERVICE_RESILIENCE_LINEAGE

  2. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

A component is ready only for a declared effect path, with current dependencies, capacity and authority—not because its process answers.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Health endpoint is green while database, DNS, queue, credential, control plane or downstream effect path is stale/unusable.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Dependencies fail independently and topology changes dynamically; local checks cover only one process.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Traffic sent to unusable instances, false completion, cascading retries and delayed diagnosis.

MECHANISM

Discover/version topology; separate liveness/readiness; exercise synthetic end-to-end paths; check dependency currentness/capacity and effect boundary; degrade by capability.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Service depends on remote components or dynamic routing/ownership.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Standalone local process with no remote effect path, where process liveness is the whole service.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Current topology, dependency contracts, credentials/routing, representative probes, consequence-safe synthetic operations and freshness bounds.

FAILURE_MODEL

Missing spans/logs, sampling bias, clock skew, telemetry delay/outage, cardinality loss, stale topology and observer effects.

TIMING_MODEL

Telemetry has acquisition/ingestion delay and clock uncertainty; currentness is bounded or marked unknown.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Telemetry consistency does not imply application-state consistency.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Source identity, configuration and topology provenance accompany observations.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Trace context/correlation may be dropped, duplicated or sampled.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Evidence survives or is intentionally preserved through failure and recovery.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Coverage, sampling, missingness and uncertainty are first-class data.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Routes and declares service based on usable capabilities rather than process existence.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Load balancer, orchestrator, operator and calling service.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Probe bypasses real dependency

  2. Probe cached

  3. Synthetic path lacks write effect

  4. Dependency topology stale

  5. Probe load worsens incident

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. End-to-end probes cost capacity and can mutate data

  2. No finite probe covers all paths

  3. Dependency awareness can create tight coupling

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From ping/process checks to capability-specific readiness, topology reconstruction and black-box effect tests.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Readiness states the exact effect path and evidence age; green does not imply every dependency or operation is healthy.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A `/health` 200 response is not service readiness.

CURRENT_STATUS

RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

VERY_HIGH

PRIMARY_SOURCES

  1. S008

  2. S064

  3. S080

  4. S081

CRITICAL_SOURCES

  1. S073

  2. S075

  3. S076

  4. S092

EMPIRICAL_OR_OUTAGE_EVIDENCE

  1. S073

  2. S075

  3. S076

CONTRARY_EVIDENCE

Formal or implementation correctness does not establish that the deployed assumptions, external effects and operational boundary satisfy the claim.

OPEN_QUESTIONS

  1. How can end-to-end probes remain representative without causing harmful side effects or excess load?

EVIDENCE_STRENGTH_PARTITION

P42 — Distributed observability treated as partial, sampled evidence
PROPERTY_ID

P42

PROPERTY_NAME

Distributed observability treated as partial, sampled evidence

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Distributed logs/metrics/traces, Dapper/X-Trace and later adaptive/retroactive tracing.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. HYBRID

  2. CLOUD_NATIVE_TRANSLATION

ORIGINAL_FORM

Telemetry helps reconstruct behaviour but carries missingness, delay, sampling and observer limits.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Complete-looking traces, stable aggregates or correlated logs are used as proof of a complete causal history or current state.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Telemetry pipelines fail, sample, reorder and lag independently from the system observed.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

False diagnosis, missed rare failure, false authority/currentness and overconfident completion claims.

MECHANISM

Record sampling/coverage/provenance; preserve high-value triggers; correlate but do not conflate clocks; combine black-box and white-box evidence; mark unknown spans/state.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Diagnosis, currentness, completion, failure-model validation or causal reconstruction depends on distributed telemetry.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

Local deterministic operation with direct authoritative state inspection.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Trace-context propagation, source identity, clock/ingestion bounds, sampling design, retention, privacy/security and telemetry-outage handling.

FAILURE_MODEL

Missing spans/logs, sampling bias, clock skew, telemetry delay/outage, cardinality loss, stale topology and observer effects.

TIMING_MODEL

Telemetry has acquisition/ingestion delay and clock uncertainty; currentness is bounded or marked unknown.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Telemetry consistency does not imply application-state consistency.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Source identity, configuration and topology provenance accompany observations.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Trace context/correlation may be dropped, duplicated or sampled.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Evidence survives or is intentionally preserved through failure and recovery.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Coverage, sampling, missingness and uncertainty are first-class data.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Makes observability useful without treating a lossy observer as omniscient.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Operator, debugger, auditor, automated detector and incident commander.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Head sample misses rare path

  2. Tail sample drops trace under overload

  3. Missing span looks like nonexecution

  4. Metric aggregate hides one unsafe operation

  5. Telemetry outage coincides with incident

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Full capture is expensive and privacy-sensitive

  2. Instrumentation changes timing

  3. Sampling strategies bias evidence

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From per-node logs to cross-layer traces, exemplars, triggered/retroactive collection and explicit coverage metadata.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Every inference states evidence source, coverage, freshness and missingness; authoritative state queries are used where available.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

A trace that looks complete is not a complete causal history.

CURRENT_STATUS

P43 — Causal trace reconstruction and event provenance
PROPERTY_ID

P43

PROPERTY_NAME

Causal trace reconstruction and event provenance

HISTORICAL_ORIGIN

Logical clocks, distributed snapshots, X-Trace/Dapper and trace-context standards.

LINEAGE_CLASS

  1. CAUSALITY_AND_TIME_LINEAGE

  2. HYBRID

  3. CONVERGENT_ENGINEERING

ORIGINAL_FORM

Reconstruct enough causal and authority provenance to explain an operation across components, not merely correlate similar timestamps.

PROBLEM_IT_ADDRESSED

Logs share IDs/times but lack parentage, message/effect identity, configuration and authority generation.

DISTRIBUTED_FAILURE_MODE

Events are reordered, sampled and emitted by processes with skewed clocks and changing topology.

FAILURE_MODE_IT_TRIES_TO_PREVENT

Incorrect root-cause order, inability to link retries/effects, and false claim that correlation proves causation.

MECHANISM

Propagate trace and operation identity, parent/links, config/term/version and event source; preserve causal edges; annotate gaps and sampled branches.

TRIGGER_OR_CONTEXT

Debugging, audit, recovery or completion requires cross-component reconstruction.

NON_TRIGGER_OR_CHEAP_PATH

One local process with deterministic logs and no remote effects.

DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS

Stable identity, context propagation, causal model, clock uncertainty, sampling metadata, source provenance and retention.

FAILURE_MODEL

Missing spans/logs, sampling bias, clock skew, telemetry delay/outage, cardinality loss, stale topology and observer effects.

TIMING_MODEL

Telemetry has acquisition/ingestion delay and clock uncertainty; currentness is bounded or marked unknown.

CONSISTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Telemetry consistency does not imply application-state consistency.

MEMBERSHIP_OR_AUTHORITY_PRECONDITIONS

Source identity, configuration and topology provenance accompany observations.

DELIVERY_OR_IDEMPOTENCY_PRECONDITIONS

Trace context/correlation may be dropped, duplicated or sampled.

RECOVERY_PRECONDITIONS

Evidence survives or is intentionally preserved through failure and recovery.

OBSERVABILITY_PRECONDITIONS

Coverage, sampling, missingness and uncertainty are first-class data.

EXPECTED_ENGINEERING_PAYOFF

Improves fault discrimination and links retries, authority changes and effects without inventing total order.

DECISION_OR_CONSUMER

Debugger, incident investigator, recovery planner and auditor.

KNOWN_FAILURE_MODES

  1. Context dropped at async boundary

  2. Retry creates unrelated trace

  3. Clock order contradicts causality

  4. Sampling removes parent

  5. Same correlation ID reused

IMPORTANT_CRITICISMS

  1. Trace context can leak data

  2. High cardinality/cost

  3. Causal provenance cannot include unobserved physical events automatically

HOW_THE_PROPERTY_EVOLVED

From timestamped logs to explicit parent/link graphs, operation/effect identity and authority/configuration provenance.

MATURE_OR_EVOLVED_FORM

Reconstruction distinguishes known causal edges, concurrent events, inferred links and missing evidence.

CEREMONY_VS_PROPERTY

Correlation IDs plus sorted timestamps are not causal proof.
